ALSO V3.236 (Anchor the page on phone — Phase 3a polish on top of V3.235. User report after the drawer ship: "*the pages on the phone feel loose, if i move to the left or the right the page moves around loose as well.*" Diagnosis: two mobile-browser defaults made the new full-bleed phone layouts feel disconnected — any descendant of `<body>` even one pixel wider than the viewport (drawer `transform: translateX(±100%)` panes, KaTeX subpixel rounding, PDF canvas elements) opened a horizontal scroll that the body could be dragged through; and iOS Safari's rubber-band bounce let vertical scroll drift past content edges. Both effects were dramatically worse on the V3.235 drawer-host pages because they're full-bleed (`.lp-page { padding: 0; max-width: none }`, `.pe-page { height: 100vh }`) with no peripheral chrome to anchor against. V3.236 adds a single `@media (max-width: 640px)` block to [rm/css/tokens.css](../../rm/css/tokens.css) that locks both axes on the `<html>` + `<body>` elements: `overflow-x: hidden` clips horizontal overflow from any descendant so the body can no longer drag sideways; `overscroll-behavior: none` kills the vertical rubber-band; `max-width: 100vw` on body is belt-and-suspenders against any legacy rule that might set a wider `min-width`. Applied to both `html` and `body` because iOS Safari historically split scroll-behavior properties between the two — the cross-browser-safe form is to apply to both. Internal scroll containers (PDF viewer at `.lp-viewer`, comment lists at `.pe-comments-list` / `.lp-annotations-inner`, chat threads, paragraph editor at `.pe-main-inner`) keep their own overflow — they're free to scroll within their box without dragging the body. The V3.235 `.rm-mobile-drawer-locked` body class still wins when a drawer is open — it adds `position: fixed; width: 100%` for full scroll arrest with the iOS-friendly scroll-position save; V3.236 rules layer underneath as the default-state behavior. `overflow-x: hidden` does NOT break `.top-nav`'s `position: sticky` because the well-known sticky-on-overflow-ancestor trap applies only when sticky is supposed to scroll within that ancestor — here the sticky is anchored to the viewport via the document scroll, and `overflow-x: hidden` doesn't create a new vertical scroll context. Tablet + desktop (≥641px) are unaffected — rule is scoped to the phone-only media block. Out of scope: per-page `touch-action` tuning, iOS pinch-zoom prevention. Plan doc: [CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.236_phone_anchor.md](../ClaudesPlan/V3.236_phone_anchor.md). `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 204 → 205.) ALSO V3.235 (Mobile side-drawer pattern — Phase 3a of the RM mobile-compatibility plan. Paper-builder editor + library-paper reader convert their left + right panes into swipe-in drawers at ≤640px while staying byte-identical above 640px. New reusable opt-in `.rm-mobile-drawer-*` pattern in [rm/css/style.css](../../rm/css/style.css) (~120 lines) defines `.rm-mobile-drawer-host` (container carrying a `data-drawer="left|right"` attribute as open state), `.rm-mobile-drawer-left` / `.rm-mobile-drawer-right` (panes that fly off-screen via `transform: translateX(±100%)` and slide back to `translateX(0)` when host's `data-drawer` matches), `.rm-mobile-drawer-backdrop` (fades in via opacity), `.rm-mobile-drawer-toggle` + `.rm-mobile-drawer-toggle-badge` (toggle buttons + count badge that are `display: none` above 640px). `var(--safe-top)` / `var(--safe-bottom)` padding handles iOS notch + home indicator; `overscroll-behavior: contain` prevents drawer-internal scroll from chaining to the page underneath. New self-contained IIFE [rm/js/mobile-drawers.js](../../rm/js/mobile-drawers.js) (~100 lines) exposes `window.MOBILE_DRAWERS.init(host, opts)` — injects the backdrop element if not present, wires backdrop click + Escape to close, toggles `data-drawer` on host, locks/unlocks body scroll with an iOS-Safari `position: fixed; top: -<scrollY>px` save/restore so the drawer doesn't propagate scroll. Idempotent: cached on host so re-init is a no-op. Returns `{ open(side), close(), toggle(side), host }`. Paper-builder ([rm/pages/paper-builder.html](../../rm/pages/paper-builder.html) editor mode): `.pe-page` gains `.rm-mobile-drawer-host`; `.pe-sidebar` (outline + paragraph mirror) gains `.rm-mobile-drawer-left`; `.pe-comments-pane` (comments list + composer) gains `.rm-mobile-drawer-right`. Two new buttons in `.pe-topbar`: `☰ Outline` (`#pe-mobile-left-toggle`) and `💬 Comments` (`#pe-mobile-right-toggle`) with a count badge. [rm/js/paper-editor.js](../../rm/js/paper-editor.js) gains `_wireMobileDrawers()` running on DOMContentLoaded; `onOpen('right')` ensures `commentsPaneEl.hidden = false` and defaults `commentsState.filter = 'view'` so the drawer is never empty even when no block is focused. `recomputeCommentCounts()` calls a new `_updateMobileRightBadge()` so the badge stays in sync (`99+` cap, `[hidden]` when zero). `closeComments()` extended to also call `host._rmMobileDrawersWired.close()` so the existing close-X inside the comments pane retracts the drawer cleanly. Library-paper ([rm/pages/library-paper.html](../../rm/pages/library-paper.html)): `.lp-split-3` gains `.rm-mobile-drawer-host`; `.lp-side` (metadata, tags, citations) gains `.rm-mobile-drawer-left`; `.lp-annotations` (PDF nav toolbar + annotation list) gains `.rm-mobile-drawer-right`. New `<div class="lp-mobile-bar">` inside `.lp-main` (above `.lp-viewer`) hosts `ℹ️ Info` toggle, centered title, `📝 Notes` toggle — hidden above 640px. [rm/js/library-paper.js](../../rm/js/library-paper.js) gains `_wireMobileDrawers()` (called from `init()` after `_wireToolbar()`) and `_updateMobileTitle(title)` (called from `_renderMetadata` so the phone toolbar shows the paper title). Phone-only library-paper CSS block in style.css overrides `.lp-page { padding: 0; max-width: none }` for full-bleed PDF, `.lp-split-3 { grid-template-columns: 1fr; height: calc(100vh - 56px - var(--safe-top)) }` so only `.lp-main` is in flow (the drawer panes are removed via `position: fixed`), hides the in-pane `.lp-collapse-btn` / `.lp-expand-btn` since drawers now toggle externally. Above 640px every drawer-related class is inert — desktop and tablet are byte-identical to V3.234. Tablet 641-1024px continues to use the V3.233 stacked layout for library-paper and the desktop 3-col layout for paper-builder; promoting drawers to tablet is a one-line breakpoint change if user feedback warrants. Side-drawers chosen over the V3.231 plan's "bottom sheet + 3-tab view" approach because they're consistent across both pages, leave the underlying content (paragraph editor / PDF) full-bleed underneath, and create a reusable opt-in pattern any future RM page with side context menus can adopt. Out of scope: touch-edge-swipe-to-open gestures (drawers open via button tap only); other Tier A pages (tasks inbox, chat, lab notebook, huddle/training/people, calendar); Tier C desktop-only interstitial. Plan doc: [CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.235_mobile_drawers.md](../ClaudesPlan/V3.235_mobile_drawers.md). `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 203 → 204.) ALSO V3.234 (Synology integration — Phase 1: structured external-links field on every relevant RM doc. Students can paste arbitrary URLs (Synology share links, Drive links, anything) onto lab-notebook entries, task project buckets, project / item cards, and paper drafts; each link carries an optional `🔌 Private (UA VPN)` toggle that surfaces a "🔌 VPN" badge — the inline acknowledgment that DSM stays off the public internet at UA so share URLs only resolve when the user is on UA VPN. Phase 2 (browser-direct Synology File Station API to auto-list `proj_{id}` folders, hostname-based auto-detection of `private:true`, DSM API token storage at `labConfig/synology`, CORS configuration on the DSM side, "Open in Synology" deep links, per-student DSM accounts) layers on top without re-doing the data model and is gated on locking in the production NAS hostname + the shared-service-account-vs-per-student credentials decision. New file [rm/js/external-links.js](../../rm/js/external-links.js) exposes `window.EXTERNAL_LINKS` with `mount({container, getLinks, setLinks, readOnly, title})` and `hydrate(arr)` — mirrors the [`section-comments.js`](../../rm/js/section-comments.js) precedent: a parameterized primitive each surface plugs into with its own persistence. Renders a collapsible "🔗 Linked files (N)" block with a list of `[label] [↗ open] [🔌 VPN?] [🗑]` rows, an add form (URL input + label input + private checkbox + Add button), and an empty-state hint. Each link is `{id, url, label, private, addedAt, addedByUid}`. `normalizeUrl` prepends `https://` when the user pastes a schemeless host; `hydrate` coerces legacy/missing fields. URL clicks open in a new tab with `rel="noopener noreferrer"`. Default collapsed iff the list is empty. Four target surfaces wired: (1) [rm/js/notebook-schemas.js](../../rm/js/notebook-schemas.js) `notebookEmptyEntry()` adds `externalLinks: []`; `notebookHydrate(d)` runs `window.EXTERNAL_LINKS.hydrate(d.externalLinks)` when the helper is loaded with a fallback to plain array coercion; [rm/js/lab-notebook.js](../../rm/js/lab-notebook.js) `renderEditor()` injects a `<div id="ln-ext-links">` placeholder after `renderRefsHtml(...)` and `wireEditor()` mounts `EXTERNAL_LINKS` wired to `state.doc.externalLinks` with the existing `scheduleSave()` autosave; owner-only surface so `readOnly: false` is hard-coded. (2) [rm/js/tasks-buckets.js](../../rm/js/tasks-buckets.js) wires the block at the **project bucket** (tier 1) level — not sub-bucket (tier 2) or subtask (tier 3) — because files are typically project-scoped and tier 1 avoids cluttering the already-heavy Subtask schema; `render()` calls a new `renderExternalLinksBlock(p)` helper between `renderTaskBuilderHead(p)` and `renderSuggestedFromCategories(p)`; skipped on the `proj-inbox` reserved task builder; the array is created lazily on first access, no schema-doc update needed. (3) [rm/js/projects.js](../../rm/js/projects.js) `renderCard(item)` (when expanded) injects a `<div data-ext-links-mount>` placeholder between `renderItemHero(item)` and `renderItemDetailAccordions(item)` and mounts `EXTERNAL_LINKS` wired to `item.externalLinks` with `saveItemSurgical(item)`; honours the existing `_canEditPaper(item)` gate so non-first-author co-authors on paper items see the block in `readOnly: true` mode; works for every item type (paper / grant / course / tool / etc.) because every item is a row in `items.json`. (4) [rm/js/paper-editor.js](../../rm/js/paper-editor.js) topbar gains a "🔗 Linked files (N)" button next to the Versions / Export-LaTeX buttons; click toggles a positioned `.ext-links-popover` rendering the shared component; backed by direct `firebridge.db().collection('items').doc(paperId)` reads/writes — **same collection as `projects.js`**, externalLinks shared between the card view and the editor as single source of truth; a `.onSnapshot` subscription keeps the count badge live across tabs and collaborators; `readOnly` mirrors `state.editable`. `items/{paperId}` was chosen over `papers/{paperId}` because the latter is the library reader collection (PDF + annotations + Yjs) and is admin-write only by rule; the paper-editor's `state.meta` loads from the legacy `projects/papers.json` route, but the canonical paper item now lives in `items.json` (V3.130 unification) where the projects.js card view writes. No new Firestore collection, no rule change — the field is purely additive on existing docs that lab members already write (`userData/{uid}/notebook/{entryId}`, `userData/{uid}/buckets/{taskBuilderId}`, `items/{itemId}`); the existing `items/{itemId}` update rule (firestore.rules:813-818) already gates writes correctly (lab member for non-paper items; first-author OR admin for paper items). Yjs left untouched on paper drafts — external links don't need CRDT semantics because they're not edited at the character level; storing on the top-level meta doc avoids the V3.230-class Y.Map-in-same-transact pitfalls. `rm/css/style.css` gains `.ext-links-*` classes (header / toggle / caret / title / count / body / empty / list / row / link / label / url / vpn / del / form / inputs / private / add) plus an `.ext-links-popover` variant used by the paper-editor topbar mount; standard lab grayscale + warm amber for the VPN badge. Host pages updated: [rm/pages/lab-notebook.html](../../rm/pages/lab-notebook.html), [rm/pages/tasks.html](../../rm/pages/tasks.html), [rm/pages/projects.html](../../rm/pages/projects.html), [rm/pages/teaching.html](../../rm/pages/teaching.html), [rm/pages/paper-builder.html](../../rm/pages/paper-builder.html) each load `external-links.js` before the renderer that consumes it. `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 202 → 203. Wires the V3.232 design tokens into the global shell ([rm/css/style.css](../../rm/css/style.css) `.page` wrapper, top-nav, dropdown menus, modals) and standardizes the 6 scattered shell-level breakpoints (640 / 700 / 720 × 3 / 800 / 860 / 880 × 2) to the two canonical values 640px (phone) and 1024px (tablet-and-below). `.page` migrated from hardcoded `max-width: 1200px; padding: 24px` to `max-width: var(--page-max-w); padding: var(--page-pad-desktop)`; a new `@media (max-width: 1024px)` block shrinks padding to `var(--page-pad-tablet)` (16px); the existing 640px block shrinks further to `var(--page-pad-mobile)` (12px). Top nav at ≤640px collapses link labels via the new `.nav-label` span pattern — every `<a>` link emitted by [rm/js/nav.js](../../rm/js/nav.js)'s `makeTopLink` now wraps the human-readable portion of its label (`"🗂 Workflows"` → icon text-node + `<span class="nav-label">Workflows</span>`) so the CSS rule `.top-nav .nav-label { display: none }` inside the 640px block leaves only the emoji icons (`🗂`, `⊞`, `⊟`) visible on phone, saving ~260px of horizontal room. `_wfPinCustomWorkflows` gets the same treatment for the `"⭐ My Workflows"` dropdown trigger; the `::after { content: ' \25BE' }` caret rule is untouched since pseudo-elements aren't affected by inserting child nodes. Nav dropdown menus at ≤640px reposition from the trigger-anchored absolute layout into a full-width slide-up bottom sheet (`position: fixed; bottom: 0; left: 0; right: 0; border-radius: 12px 12px 0 0; padding: var(--sp-2) 0 calc(var(--safe-bottom) + var(--sp-2)); z-index: var(--z-drawer)`) with `var(--tap-min-compact)` 36px tap targets and `max-height: 70vh` so the sheet scrolls inside the viewport. Three core modal classes — `.modal` (universal), `.pin-modal` (pin pickers on the dashboard + tasks page), `.ev-modal` (event modal on calendar) — get `var(--safe-top) + var(--safe-bottom)` subtracted from their `max-height` so close buttons stay reachable above the iOS home indicator on notched phones. ALSO V3.232 (Mobile/tablet design tokens — Phase 1 of the broader RM mobile-compatibility plan. New [rm/css/tokens.css](../../rm/css/tokens.css) defines a single source of truth on `:root` for spacing (`--sp-1` 4px through `--sp-10` 40px, 8-pt grid), font scale (`--fs-xs` 11px through `--fs-3xl` 28px, kept in px to avoid churning 21 sibling CSS files), breakpoint values (`--bp-phone` 640px, `--bp-tablet` 1024px, `--bp-desktop` 1280px — reference-only because CSS custom properties cannot be used inside `@media` queries; subsequent phases use literal pixel values in their queries and standardize on the two values 640px / 1024px), touch sizing (`--tap-min` 44px per Apple HIG / WCAG 2.5.5, `--tap-min-compact` 36px), page padding (`--page-pad-mobile` 12px / `--page-pad-tablet` 16px / `--page-pad-desktop` 24px), `--page-max-w` 1200px, iOS safe-area-inset variables (`--safe-top/bottom/left/right`), and a z-index ladder (`--z-nav` 100 through `--z-toast` 1500). Three global behavior fixes ship alongside the tokens: `input, select, textarea { font-size: max(16px, var(--fs-base)) }` prevents iOS Safari from auto-zooming when an input gets focus; `button, .btn, .top-nav a, .dropdown-menu a { touch-action: manipulation }` eliminates the 300ms tap delay on touch devices; an `@media (hover: none)` block suppresses sticky-highlight states on `.card` / `.top-nav a` / `.dropdown-menu a` / `.yr-line` / `.ch-line` / `.lib-line` (the rules most likely to "stick" after a tap on a touch device), pairing with explicit `:active` states added in later phases. `@supports (padding: env(safe-area-inset-top))` pads `body` with `--safe-top` / `--safe-bottom` so the notch / home-indicator don't clip content on notched iOS devices; non-iOS browsers are unaffected. Load order on every page is `tokens.css` → `style.css` → page-specific CSS. The link tag `<link rel="stylesheet" href="/rm/css/tokens.css">` was inserted immediately before the existing `style.css` link on 66 files — every `rm/pages/*.html` that loads `style.css` plus [rm/index.html](../../rm/index.html); 14 redirect-stub pages (activity-tracker / tasks-inbox / tasks-archive / tasks-add / year-review / paper-editor / library-comments / library-public / important-people / funding / email-review / cv-overview / activity-summary / purchase-requests) load no CSS at all and were skipped. Zero behavior change vs V3.231 — tokens are defined but no existing CSS references them yet; downstream phases (Phase 2: shell adaptation, Phase 3: Tier A page rewrites, Phase 4: Tier C interstitial) consume them. Plan doc: [CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.232_mobile_tokens.md](../ClaudesPlan/V3.232_mobile_tokens.md). Full mobile plan: `~/.claude/plans/i-want-to-make-rosy-scott.md`. `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 200 → 201.) ALSO V3.231 (Paper Export Workspace replaces the prompt-driven LaTeX dialog. The Export LaTeX button on a paper now navigates to a new page at [rm/pages/paper-export.html](../../rm/pages/paper-export.html) — pick a journal template, toggle a curated set of LaTeX add-ons (lineno / setspace-double / microtype / cleveref / hyperref-colors / anonymize / placeins / twocolumn), override figure / table / equation defaults via per-paper style knobs, run pre-flight lints (dangling \\ref / missing bib / float-order / shell-escape), and either compile to PDF on a Tectonic-backed Cloud Run service or download a self-contained .zip for local `latexmk` compilation. Compile config persists at the shared `paperExports/{paperId}` Firestore doc gated by `isLabContributor()` write / `isLabMember()` read so coauthors see the same journal target; compile history lives in the `paperExports/{paperId}/compiles/{compileId}` subcollection so the parent doc stays small. The Cloud Run service is scaffolded at [functions/paper-compile/](../../functions/paper-compile/) with a Tectonic 0.15.0 + biber + chktex Dockerfile and an Express `server.js` that verifies the Firebase ID-token bearer header, mirrors `isLabMember()`, runs Tectonic in a per-compile `/tmp` workdir with a path-traversal guard, and uploads the produced PDF to Firebase Storage at `paperExports/{paperId}/compiles/{compileId}.pdf` (admin-SDK write bypasses Storage rules); the client-side `PAPER_COMPILE_URL` constant in [rm/js/paper-export.js](../../rm/js/paper-export.js) is null until the service is deployed, so the Compile button shows a "service not configured" panel pointing users at the .zip path. Tectonic chosen over full TeX Live for the engine: ~50 MB binary vs ~2 GB image, reproducible CTAN auto-fetch, faster cold start; the 8-entry add-on registry is Tectonic-safe (no `minted` / shell-escape needed). `rm/js/paper-export-tex.js` gains `buildBundle({paperId, templateId|template, overrides, addons, yDoc, citationByKey, fullItemsByKey, leadAuthor, titleOverride})` as the pure no-I/O bundle builder used by both the cloud-compile path (POST .tex + .bib + figures bundle to the Cloud Run service) and the local-disk path (.zip via JSZip); `mergeTemplateWithOverrides(template, overrides)` deep-merges per-paper figure/table/equation defaults over the template before rendering; `applyAddons(basePreamble, addonIds, templateId)` composes the final preamble. `ADDON_REGISTRY` is the single source of truth — both the build pipeline and the UI consume it. PDF preview reuses the existing `window.PDF_VIEWER.create(container, url)` API from [rm/js/pdf-viewer.js](../../rm/js/pdf-viewer.js) — no fork. Persistence reads/writes via direct `firebridge.db().collection('paperExports').doc(paperId)` refs since the adapter routes table only matches exact paths (same precedent as `papers/{paperId}/yjs_*` and PMR cross-user reads). `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 199 → 200. Phase 2 — out of scope here — covers the lab-wide `.cls` / `.bst` upload library at Storage path `journal-styles/{slug}/*`, in-service figure fetch from `paper-figures/{paperId}/*` to resolve `\\includegraphics{}` references, scheduled TTL cleanup of compile artifacts, `latexdiff` revision tracking, and the scitex-writer-inspired IMRAD writing-guideline panel. Plan: `CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.231_paper_export_workspace.md`.) ALSO V3.230 (Fix paper creation crash on template seed. Right after V3.229 shipped, creating a paper through the new one-click research-view flow crashed in `appendSectionFromTree` at [rm/js/paper-editor.js](../../rm/js/paper-editor.js) with `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'insert')` on the `m.get('label').insert(0, sec.label)` line. Root cause was a latent Yjs 13 quirk: when a Y.Map is itself freshly integrated within the current transact, `set(key, new Y.Text(''))` followed by `get(key)` doesn't reliably return the just-set Y.Text in the same transact — `get` returns undefined and the `.insert` call crashes. The seed-template path only runs on first connect to a brand-new paper, so the bug was latent until V3.229's one-click create flow made it easy to reach. The composer is now wrapped in a `<div class="pe-comments-new">` block with a `<div class="pe-comments-new-title">New comment</div>` label; the V3.210 `<div class="pe-comments-anchor-preview">` moved inside this wrapper between the title and the textarea so the snippet is visually attached to the comment being authored. `_installCommentSelectionWatcher`'s `selectionchange` handler branches on `!commentsPaneEl.hidden`: when open, hides the floating button, switches `commentsState.activeBlockId` to the selection's block (if different — auto-retargets the pane to the highlighted paragraph), refreshes context label + list + composer state, and assigns `commentsState.pendingAnchor = {anchorStart, anchorEnd, anchorText}` so `_refreshAnchorPreview()` paints the snippet directly above the textbox. When closed, the V3.210 floating-button flow runs unchanged. View-mode doesn't re-render the list on block switch because the list already shows everything in view — only the composer's target changes. Floating button stays the discovery affordance for first-time users; once the pane is open the user's gaze stays in the pane. New CSS: `.pe-comments-new` (sticky-bottom flex column), `.pe-comments-new-title`, `.pe-comments-new .pe-comments-anchor-preview` margin/border override. `.pe-comments-composer` border-top removed. No data-model changes. `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 180 → 181.)

V3.210 (Paper builder comments: highlight-anchored comments + filter toggle between "this paragraph" and "paragraphs in view". User can now highlight text in a paragraph body, see a floating "💬 Comment" button anchored to the selection's bottom-left, click it to stage the source-space `(anchorStart, anchorEnd, anchorText)` triple, open the comments pane pre-filled with the snippet, and post — the resulting comment doc carries the three new optional fields on top of the legacy `blockId` and renders a yellow-left-bordered italic snippet above the comment body in the card. A new `<select id="pe-comments-filter">` in the pane header toggles between `block` (legacy: show comments for the active paragraph only) and `view` (compute `_visibleBlockIds()` on demand via `getBoundingClientRect()` + viewport, group results by `blockId` under clickable `.pe-comments-block-header` jump-to-block headings). Filter persists via `localStorage.rm_paper_comments_filter`. A 120 ms-debounced `scroll` listener refreshes view-mode results as the user scrolls. `commentsState` gains `filter` + `pendingAnchor`. `_charOffsetFromContainer(rootEl, container, offset)` is the V3.191 chip-aware walker minus the Selection-API dependency so source-space offsets stay consistent. `openCommentsForBlock(blockId, label, anchor)` accepts a third arg; `submitComment` folds in the staged anchor as three doc fields (`anchorText` capped at 500 chars); `closeComments` clears the staging. Composer disabled in view-mode when no `activeBlockId` is set with a placeholder hint. New CSS: `.pe-comments-filterbar`, `.pe-comments-anchor-preview` (amber strip with clear-`×` button), `.pe-comment-anchor-snippet` (per-card amber-left-border snippet), `.pe-comments-scope-label` + `.pe-comments-block-header`, `.pe-comment-floating-btn` (absolute-positioned blue pill, z-index 4200). Anchor offsets are a snapshot — not CRDT pointers — so they can become stale on subsequent edits; the stored `anchorText` keeps the comment legible regardless. No new Firestore rules; the new fields ride on the existing `papers/{paperId}/comments/{commentId}` doc shape. `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 179 → 180.)

V3.209 (New user category `collaborator` for external partners. Added to `PEOPLE_CAT_ORDER` and `PEOPLE_CAT_LABEL` in [rm/js/people.js](rm/js/people.js) (positioned between `alumni` and `guest`) and to `CATEGORY_ORDER` in [rm/js/equipment.js](rm/js/equipment.js). Available in the Edit Lab Profile form, the Role form, and the Invite form. `LAB_USERS.isVisible(u)` extended to treat `u.category === 'collaborator'` the same as the V3.208 `hidden` flag — auto-filtered from every internal directory for non-admin viewers; admins still see them everywhere. Public `#/team` filter in [app.js](app.js) gains `&& u.category !== 'collaborator'`. The substantive new behavior is in `canAccessPage(pageId)`: collaborators have **no implicit default-workflow baseline** — admin must explicitly grant every page via `userPermissions/{uid}.allowedPageIds` through the existing `/rm/pages/admin-permissions.html` matrix. The function now branches between the explicit-grant check and the implicit baseline: if no grant exists AND the profile's category is `collaborator`, return false; otherwise fall through to the PAGE_REGISTRY.defaultPageIdSet baseline. This is the "enhanced access via the matrix" rule — collaborators see only what admin ticks. `canAccessPage` is a UI gate (workflow chip strips + page discovery), not a security boundary; firestore.rules still gates by role only, so a collaborator who knows a URL can still load most pages. Tightening firestore.rules to honor per-user `allowedPageIds` is deferred. `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 178 → 179. Plan: `CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.209_collaborator_category.md`.) ALSO V3.208 (New optional `hidden: boolean` field on `users/{uid}` lets admins mark any lab member — PI, postdoc, grad, undergrad, highschool, alumni — as off-directory without revoking RM access. Works the same for every category; the flag is exposed as a checkbox on both the `Role` form and the `Edit Lab Profile` form in [rm/js/people.js](rm/js/people.js). Hidden users keep full lab-member access (sign in, dashboard, get assigned tasks); they're just filtered out of every people-list except for admins. Centralizes the visibility predicate in a new `window.LAB_USERS.isVisible(u)` helper at the bottom of `rm/js/firebase-bridge.js`: returns `true` iff `u.role && u.role !== 'guest'` AND (`!u.hidden` OR the signed-in viewer's profile has `role === 'admin'`). 14 RM people-list call sites swap their old inline `u.role && u.role !== 'guest'` filter to the helper: `pmr.js` (left rail), `task-assign.js`, `meetings.js`, `protocol-editor.js`, `activity-summary.js`, `grant-facilities.js`, `lab-tasks.js`, `tasks-buckets.js`, `sharing.js`, `procurement.js`, `dashboard.js` (3 spots), `chat.js`, `cv-overview.js`. Parent SPA `app.js#/team` filter at line 567 doesn't load firebridge, so it inlines `u.role !== 'guest' && !u.hidden` — the public site never has an admin viewer so the helper's admin-bypass branch is irrelevant there. `rm/js/people.js` gains a `hidden` field in `LAB_FIELDS` (Edit Lab Profile form) and in the `changeRole` form so admins can flip it at promotion time alongside role + category; row render shows a small "hidden" chip when set so admins notice; counts on the filter tabs and the rendered list both apply the admin-bypass so non-admin viewers of the people page itself don't see hidden rows. No firestore.rules change — `users/{uid}` remains publicly readable (the public team page still needs to read it), so `hidden` is a UI display filter rather than a privacy boundary; the user's stated goal is "don't release them on pages or lists," not "encrypt them away from determined clients," so this matches the ask. `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 177 → 178. Plan: `CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.208_hidden_user_flag.md`.) ALSO V3.205 (Console-error sweep across the RM tree. Four issues caught in the browser console after the V3.204 ship resolved: (1) `compliance.js:104` crashed with `Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'length')` on first-load for any user whose IRB/IACUC tab returned a Firestore-empty doc — `loadAndRender` now reads `const items = (data && data[tab.dataKey]) || []` so the empty-state path renders cleanly; (2) `firestore.indexes.json` `tasks.bucket` `fieldOverrides` entry only listed `COLLECTION_GROUP` scope, which silently dropped the default single-field `COLLECTION` index — every subcollection query of the form `userData/{uid}/tasks where('bucket','==', X)` (inbox/daily/weekly/monthly/annual/assigned) returned `failed-precondition`; added the missing `COLLECTION_ASC` index alongside the existing collection-group one; (3) `firestore.rules` had no document-level rule for the top-level `papers` collection (only its subcollections — annotations / yjs_* / blocks / snapshots / comments) so PMR's `collection('papers').where('authorUids','array-contains', uid)` paper-drafts query in `pmr.js:476` returned `permission-denied` on every render; added `match /papers/{paperId}` with read allowed for admin OR caller whose uid is in the doc's `authorUids[]`, writes admin-only until the metadata-sync from `data/projects/papers.json` lands; (4) `training.css` `.arc-controls input[type="range"]` used the deprecated `appearance: slider-vertical` (and `-webkit-appearance` form), generating a Chrome `[Deprecation]` warning per slider per render — replaced with the standardized `writing-mode: vertical-lr; direction: rtl;` pattern. Rules + indexes change requires `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules,firestore:indexes`. `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 174 → 175. No new files, no data-model changes.) ALSO V3.203 (Paper builder sidebar gets a "Paragraph" tab that auto-mirrors the focused block's skeleton bullets. Sidebar `<aside class="pe-sidebar">` restructured with a `.pe-sidebar-tabs` nav (Outline / Paragraph) above two `.pe-sidebar-tab-content` panels. Outline panel keeps the legacy `<ul#pe-outline>` + `+ Add section`. Paragraph panel mounts `<div#pe-paragraph-skel>` — a read-only mirror filled via three new private helpers in paper-editor.js: `_findBlockMapById(blockId)` walks `paperMap.sections → children`; `_updateSidebarParagraphMirror(el)` resolves the `.pe-block[data-block-id]` ancestor and rotates the `observeDeep` subscription from the previous block's `skeleton_items` Y.Array to the new one; `_renderSidebarParagraphSkeleton(blockMap, skItems)` paints a block-kind header + `<ul>` of bullets with depth-based indent (8 + depth*14 px), `⚑` flag span when `claim_id` is set, and a click handler that scrolls + focuses the matching in-doc bullet via `[data-block-id] [data-item-id] .pe-skeleton-bullet`. `rememberFocus(el)` (V3.40 caret-tracker) now calls `_updateSidebarParagraphMirror(el)` so the mirror swaps the instant the caret enters a new block. Active tab persists via `localStorage.rm_paper_sidebar_tab` (`outline` default; `paragraph` restored on next mount). New CSS: `.pe-sidebar-tabs` (sticky-top flex with bottom-border active state), `.pe-sidebar-tab` + `.active`, `.pe-sidebar-tab-content[hidden]`, `.pe-sidebar-empty` placeholder, `.pe-sidebar-skel-header` (uppercase 10px gray kind label), `.pe-sidebar-skel-item` (hover light-gray with left-blue accent), `.pe-sidebar-skel-item-claim` (amber background to surface flagged bullets), `.pe-sidebar-skel-flag`, `.pe-sidebar-skel-text`, `.pe-sidebar-skel-empty-bullet`. `.pe-sidebar` switches to flex-column without wholesale padding so the tab nav sticks to the top edge. `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 172 → 173. No data-model changes; reads only the in-memory `paperMap` Y.Doc.)

V3.201 (Paper-writing series gains a peer-review &amp; revision training — V3.188, the 8th and final entry. Both sides of the review transaction: what reviewers do (six jobs — rigor / novelty / completeness / writing / unstated-limitations / suggested-additions, plus the actual read-order they use), what authors owe in response (point-by-point, every comment, manuscript-referenced, respectful tone), three canonical response shapes (agree / partial / disagree with worked-example templates), and the high-stakes discipline of disagreeing without making it personal. [`rm/pages/training-paper-peer-review.html`](../../rm/pages/training-paper-peer-review.html) (~430 lines) is lecture-style with two interactive widgets: (1) **Reviewer-comment classifier** — paste a comment, regex heuristics across 6 categories (methodological / statistical / literature / interpretation / stylistic / scope-creep), surfaces a category-specific response-template paragraph; ships with 5 preset examples per category. (2) **Response-letter builder capstone** — form (shape + comment + response + change + reference), "Add" appends to a stacking preview rendering the canonical italicized-comment / response format with opening phrasing keyed by shape, "Clear all" resets. Tone checker regex-flags 9 defensive phrasings (`the reviewer has misunderstood`, `obviously`, `as we clearly stated`, etc.) with "we may have presented this poorly" reframing. Warnings on disagreements without manuscript refs, agreements without specific changes, responses under 30 chars. [`rm/js/training-paper-peer-review.js`](../../rm/js/training-paper-peer-review.js) (~370 lines) holds the CATEGORY_RULES table + PRESETS + DEFENSIVE_WORDS regex bank + the stacking-responses state. `rm/js/training.js` gains a tenth Foundations MODULES entry (slug `paper-peer-review`, position 10 — right after V3.187 Abstract). Foundations 10/10. Total curriculum: 16 of 21 modules live (76%). `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 171 → 172. Plan: `CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.188_training_paper_peer_review.md`.)

V3.200 (Training section relocated under Operations in `nav.js`. The standalone `Training` top-level NAV_ITEMS group (with 27 children — landing + 26 individual training slugs) is gone, replaced by a single `Training` entry as the first child of `Operations`. Operations grows 5 → 6 children; top-level NAV_ITEMS group count drops 7 → 6 (Dashboard / Activity / Research / Operations / Lab Admin / Settings). Individual training pages remain on disk and are discoverable through the landing page's categorized grid at `/rm/pages/training.html` (foundations / using-rm / microscopy / analysis / tools — 15 of 20 cards Available after V3.199). Direct URLs to individual trainings still work; they just no longer appear in the All Pages tile grid or in workflow-strip "+ Add page" pickers. No changes to `rm/js/training.js` or any per-training file. `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 169 → 170.)

V3.199 (Paper-writing series complete — all 5 section-specific trainings shipped. V3.197 set up the series + built V3.182 paragraphs/voice/scope as the foundation; V3.199 ships V3.183 Introduction + V3.184 Methods + V3.185 Results + V3.186 Discussion + V3.187 Abstract. Foundations category is now 9/9. Each section module reuses V3.182's four-voice + paragraph-skeleton vocabulary; they cross-link back, they don't re-teach. V3.183 Introduction: 3-paragraph funnel with compare-bars for ¶1 and ¶2, last-paragraph builder capstone (3 inputs → "Here we show..." Storyteller-voice elevator). V3.184 Methods: Methods-vs-Intro compare-bar + 12-item completeness checklist (cells / antibodies / reagents / equipment / acquisition / sample prep / analysis / stats / data / code / versions / supplementary). V3.185 Results: Reporter-vs-Advocate compare-bar + figure-alignment checker capstone (paste a paragraph; regex highlights advocate words red inline, flags figure-citation gaps amber on quantitative claims, checks inline statistics). V3.186 Discussion: 7-step limitation honesty meter (over-claimed → over-hedged with target zone 3–5) + 12-item four-jobs coverage checklist (interpret / situate / limit / project + sub-items). V3.187 Abstract: structured-vs-unstructured compare-bar + word-budget tool (paste Abstract → cue-word classifier splits sentences into motivation/method/result/impact, compares to 20/20/40/20 target, ceiling-violation + skewed-budget warnings). `rm/js/training.js` flips all 5 `available` flags true; plan docs V3.183–V3.187 status-marked BUILT. Foundations 9/9. Total curriculum: 15 of 20 modules live (75%). `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 168 → 169.)

V3.197 (Paper-writing training expanded into a 7-part series under Foundations. V3.163's single "Writing a scientific paper" entry is retitled "Paper writing — overview" and becomes the gateway to a per-section series: V3.183 Introduction / V3.184 Methods / V3.185 Results / V3.186 Discussion / V3.187 Abstract, all plan-only. V3.182 ships as the foundation prototype the section trainings reference back to — "Paper writing — paragraphs, voice & scope" teaches the paragraph skeleton (claim/evidence/link + section variants observation→quantification→conclusion / context→gap→what-next / interpretation→evidence→implication / what→how→why), the four voices (Reporter for Results, Storyteller for Intro+Abstract, Advocate for Discussion, Technician for Methods), narrative scope (10-level slider showing the same project rendered at each scope from "Cancer kills millions" to "BT-549 spheroids in 1.5 vs 4.5 kPa with StarDist edge segmentation"), and the zoom-out-without-losing-focus techniques (callback / implication ladder / comparison anchor). [`rm/pages/training-paper-paragraphs.html`](../../rm/pages/training-paper-paragraphs.html) (~480 lines) has four interactive widgets — skeleton↔prose transformer with 4 worked examples + skeleton/prose toggle, voice grid (static 2×2 with color-coded left borders per voice), scope dial with 10-step slider + per-step where-it-belongs labels, and paragraph builder capstone (section+voice+skeleton+3 inputs → voice-specific transition-template paragraph + voice/section mismatch warnings). [`rm/js/training-paper-paragraphs.js`](../../rm/js/training-paper-paragraphs.js) (~330 lines) holds the `EXAMPLES`/`SCOPE_LEVELS`/`RECOMMENDED` data tables + the `renderParagraph(voice, l1, l2, l3)` voice-specific paragraph composer with 4 transition templates (Reporter concatenates, Storyteller inserts "However...Here", Advocate prepends "Our results suggest that...", Technician concatenates terse). New CSS blocks in `rm/css/training.css`: `.skeleton-stage`/`.stage-nav`/`.skel-skeleton`/`.skel-prose`, `.voice-grid`/`.voice-card{.is-reporter|.is-storyteller|.is-advocate|.is-technician}`, `.scope-dial`/`.scope-meter`/`.scope-example`, `.para-output`. Foundations category grows 3 → 9 modules (PhD triangle / Scientific communication / Paper-writing × 7). nav.js Training group gains 6 children. 10 of 20 curriculum modules now live (50%). `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 166 → 167. Plan: `CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.182_training_paper_paragraphs.md` + 5 queued V3.183–V3.187.)

V3.196 (Citation popover surfaces the paragraph's claims + linked highlights. When the popover opens from a paragraph (toolbar `+ Cite`, Cmd+K, or click-to-edit on an existing cite chip), it walks the block's `skeleton_items` Y.Array for `claim_id` pointers, looks up each claim via the V3.104 `PaperBulletClaims.getClaim` local cache, collects `supporting_evidence_ids` + `counter_evidence_ids`, resolves each linked paper to its library citation_key via `state.citationByKey` (V3.40 index keyed by itemId), and Firestore-fetches each annotation doc at `papers/{paperId}/annotations/{annId}` in one batched `Promise.all` to grab the highlight text. The popover renders a sky-blue "Claims for this paragraph" section above the search list with one `.pe-cite-pop-claim-card` per claim (statement + status pill), each card listing the linked papers as checkbox-rows sharing the V3.157 multi-pick `selectedKeys` Set, and each row carrying an expand arrow `▶`/`▼` that toggles a `.pe-cite-pop-claim-evidence` sub-block listing the annotation's `target.pages[*].selectors.textQuote.exact` quote + stance pill + optional `comment`. New private helpers in paper-editor.js: `_blockIdOfParagraph` / `_claimIdsForBlock` / `_loadClaimContext` / `_fetchAnnotation` / `_refreshClaimsBar`. `openCitationPopover` gains `opts.paragraphEl` + four new `citationPopoverState` fields (`paragraphEl`, `claimContext`, `claimContextLoading`, `expandedClaimEvidence`) cleared on `hide()`. Cmd+K and toolbar `+ Cite` both wired to pass the paragraph. No new Firestore rules / indexes — reads only the V3.103 lab-readable highlight path. No schema changes. `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 163 → 164.)

V3.194 (Tasks ↔ PMR sync bridge. New `rm/js/task-pmr-sync.js` (~250 lines) exposes `window.TASK_PMR_SYNC` with `addSubtaskToCurrentWeek`, `syncSubtaskDoneToPMR`, `syncPMRItemDoneToSubtask`. Direct-Firestore refs (`firebridge.db().collection('userData').doc(uid).collection('pmr|buckets').doc(...)`) so the helper is independent of either page's lazy route registrations. `addSubtaskToCurrentWeek` picks the most-recently-updated PMR period from `_index`, finds the week containing today (auto-creates a Mon–Sun week if none matches; auto-creates "Experimental update" if no subsections), preferentially picks "Reading/Writing" for writing-category buckets, pushes an item with `source_subtask_id`. Idempotent across all subsections. `syncSubtaskDoneToPMR` scans `weekly_entries[*].subsections[*].items[]` and flips matching `status` to done/in_progress. `syncPMRItemDoneToSubtask` walks all bucket docs + children-recursive lookup to flip the source subtask's done flag. `rm/js/tasks-buckets.js` renderSubtaskRow gains a `＋ PMR` button + all three done-toggle paths (checkbox, inline Mark complete, inline Undo) fire syncSubtaskDoneToPMR. `rm/js/pmr.js` renderItem status-change handler crosses the done line + fires syncPMRItemDoneToSubtask when item carries source_subtask_id. Both pages now load `task-pmr-sync.js` after `task-assign.js`. Scope: current-period only (older PMR archives may diverge); owner-only (admin viewing another student's PMR can't reach their buckets per rules). `sw.js` `CACHE_VERSION` 161 → 162. Plan: [`CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.194_task_pmr_sync.md`](../ClaudesPlan/V3.194_task_pmr_sync.md). ALSO V3.193: PMR polish — four user-reported issues fixed in one ship: (1) reference links auto-import from notebook/protocols/paper-drafts/past-PMRs/grant-drafts, (2) sections rearrangeable via `▲`/`▼` arrows that mutate a new `state.doc.section_order: string[]`, (3) every archivable section gains a "new" + "archive" portion via a uniform `discussed_at` field + shared `splitArchive`/`renderItemArchive`/`buildDiscussedToggle` helpers — archive renders as a collapsed `<details>` with a free-text search input, (4) Goals section switched to bullet-list contentEditable (`<ul><li>…</li></ul>` root, `Tab`/`Shift+Tab` indent/outdent, Enter creates new bullet via browser default). New helpers: `effectiveSectionOrder()` merges `state.doc.section_order` with `DEFAULT_SECTION_ORDER` so future-added sections surface for older docs; `coerceGoalHtmlToList` normalizes legacy plain-text goal bodies into list shape on load. `renderBody` rewritten to iterate `effectiveSectionOrder()` via a `SECTION_RENDERERS` map + decorate each block's H2 with reorder arrows. Archive pattern applied to barriers / reference_links / project_overview / weekly_entries / discussion / figure_reviews / paper_writing — paper_writing keeps `discussed_at` on `state.doc.paper_writing[]` entries keyed by `paper_id` since drafts are pulled via live Firestore query. All splice handlers updated to use `array.indexOf(item)` fallbacks since split-rendering breaks the old numeric idx contract. Read-only-view guards inherited everywhere — admin viewing a student doesn't reorder, doesn't auto-import, doesn't toggle discussed. `sw.js` `CACHE_VERSION` 160 → 161. Plan: [`CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.193_pmr_polish_4issues.md`](../ClaudesPlan/V3.193_pmr_polish_4issues.md). ALSO V3.191: Paper builder paragraphs stay in chip-mode while focused — raw `[@…]` / `[!fig:…]` / `[!eqi:…]` / `[!eqr:…]` / `[!tab:…]` tokens never visible. Pre-V3.191 the contenteditable's focus handler swapped to plain source text so the user could edit, exposing every bracket token as raw text. V3.191 keeps the rendered chips visible even when focused: every chip span gains `contenteditable="false"` + a uniform `data-src="<original-token>"` attribute, so the caret flows around them as atomic units. New `renderChipsOnlyHtml(source)` renderer wraps bracket tokens in atomic chips while leaving markdown source (`**bold**`, `*italic*`, `~sub~`, `^sup^`, `` `code` ``) and inline math source (`$…$`) as raw text — the user only asked to hide bracket tokens. New `serializeChipDom(el)` walker reads back: text nodes contribute their nodeValue, chip elements contribute their `data-src`, `<br>` contributes `'\n'`. The bindYTextToContentEditable focus handler / input handler / Y.Text observer all switch from `el.textContent` reads/writes to `renderChipsOnlyHtml` + `serializeChipDom`. Picker insertions (`+ Cite` / `+ Eq` / `+ Fig` / `+ Table` / `+ Inline Eq` / Cmd+K) detect bracket tokens and insert as chip DOM nodes via `range.insertNode(chipNode)` rather than `execCommand('insertText')` so the chip renders immediately. All four char-offset helpers (`readCaretCharOffset` / `setCaretCharOffset` in paper-editor.js; `_readCharOffset` / `_rangeFromCharOffset` in paper-equations.js) rewritten chip-aware: atomic chips contribute `data-src.length` bytes (not `textContent.length`) so caret offsets stay in source-space. V3.157 chip-edit replaceParagraphRange also rewritten chip-aware + inserts replacement as chip DOM. Y.Text source is unchanged (same Pandoc tokens); the chip-mode is a visual layer only. Markdown source still visible while focused (user complained specifically about bracket tokens). Click-to-edit on citation chips (V3.157) still works; fig/tab/eqr/eqi chips still scroll-to-block on click (edit popovers for them deferred). Manual typing of raw `[@…]` tokens stays as raw text until next focus cycle. `sw.js` `CACHE_VERSION` 158 → 159. No data-model changes.)

V3.184.2 (Camera-selection training: Andor lineup refreshed + catalog expanded 24 → 28 cameras. Third pass after the user pointed at `andor.oxinst.com/products/scmos-for-life-science` and noted Andor wasn't reflected at current branding. Two issues: (1) Andor rebranded — "Zyla 4.2 PLUS" is now sold as **ZL41 Cell 4.2**, "Sona 4.2B-11" is now **Sona-11 32 mm**; (2) lineup grew four 2024-2026 variants. [`rm/js/training-camera-selection.js`](../../rm/js/training-camera-selection.js) `CATALOG` renames the two pre-existing Andor entries (and refines `ZL41 Cell 4.2` read noise 1.1 → 0.9 e- and USB-3.0 fps 100 → 53 — the 100 fps figure was the Camera Link mode, not the USB-3.0 mode the lab would actually use). **Four new Andor entries:** **ZL41 Cell 5.5** ($19k, 5.5 MP front-illum, 64% QE, 0.9 e- rolling / 2.3 e- global — rare global-shutter option for tracking + machine-vision-style work); **Sona-11 (22 mm)** ($20k, 2 MP, 11 µm BSI, 95% QE, 70 fps — smaller-sensor Sona-11 variant); **Sona Extreme** ($28k, 4.2 MP 6.5 µm BSI, 95% QE, sub-1 e- read noise, -45 °C deep cooling, 43 fps USB 3.0 / 135 fps CoaXPress — flagship deep-cooled BSI); **CB2 24B** ($32k, 24.5 MP BSI 2.74 µm pixels, 75% QE, 1.3 e- read noise, 106 fps full-res / 386 fps binned via CoaXPress + 10 GigE — high-res high-speed for developmental biology, requires a frame grabber so not plug-and-play USB). Catalog `fps` field intentionally reports the USB-3.0 figure (or full-frame if interface is irrelevant) rather than the headline CoaXPress / Camera Link number — USB-3.0 is the plug-and-play default on the Ti-E / Ti2-U, and the catalog's scoring is consequently fair across vendors. Tab 1 worked-example + Tab 5 capstone background updated to "28 cameras"; Tab 5's vendor list now spells out the full Andor lineup. `sw.js` `CACHE_VERSION` 157 → 158. Plan: [`CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.184_training_camera_selection.md`](../ClaudesPlan/V3.184_training_camera_selection.md).)

V3.190 (Paper-reader citation finder — additional styles + diagnostic improvements. Follow-up to V3.188. User reported two papers still misbehave: a Springer CMLS paper (`10.1007/s00018-025-06036-6`) shows zero citations despite the journal's stated `[N]` bracket style, and a Frontiers in Molecular Biosciences paper (`10.3389/fmolb.2022.998475`) surfaces only ~half its cites. V3.190 ships three targeted fixes in [`rm/js/paper-citations.js`](../../rm/js/paper-citations.js) plus a stronger diagnostic so the CMLS root cause can be pinpointed from a console paste: (a) **always-on per-page diagnostic** — `_scanPage` now logs `console.info('[paper-citations] p.N matches: …')` for EVERY page including zero-match pages (which log `(none)`); V3.188 gated this on `≥1 match || debug`, the wrong polarity for diagnosing why a paper produces zero citations; (b) **debug text dump** — `window.__PAPER_CITATIONS_DEBUG__ = true` additionally dumps the first ~400 chars of the page's joined text layer plus `medianH` / `items.length`, revealing what the inline tokens actually look like (bracket vs paren vs superscript, ASCII vs unicode); (c) **author particle prefixes** — new `_PARTICLE_SRC` prepended to `_SURNAME_SRC` allowing 0-2 lowercase particles (van, von, der, den, de, del, della, le, la, du, ten, ter, d'/d') before the capitalised surname core; catches `(van der Berg, 2020)`, `(de Vries, 2019)`, `(le Carré, 2020)`. Greedy-with-backtrack on 0-2 repeats; overreach into body prose bounded because particle-surnames only fire inside `_scanAuthorYearGroup` / `_scanNarrative`, both gated by a following year; (d) **3+ author cites** — `_AUTHOR_GROUP_SRC`'s second branch rewritten as `(?:\s*,\s*SURNAME)*\s*,?\s*(?:and|&)\s+SURNAME`. Catches `(Smith, Jones, and Williams, 2021)`, `(Murray, Lopez, Wang, and Chen, 2022)` and any longer chain; 1- and 2-author cites still work via the 0-iteration path. Deferred until console feedback arrives: unusual bracket codepoints (`〔〕`, `［］`, `⟨⟩`) — risk of false positives is too high to add speculatively. `sw.js` `CACHE_VERSION` 156 → 157. Plan: [`CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.190_citation_extra_styles.md`](../ClaudesPlan/V3.190_citation_extra_styles.md). ALSO V3.189: PMR bucket lifecycle: mark complete + archive. Closes the wishlist's "PMR bucket lifecycle" item. Pre-V3.189 a bucket whose every subtask was `done` stayed in PMR's Open Tasks rollup forever (rollup only filtered `!done` subtasks, so it just silently emptied out the bucket) and stayed in the Tasks-page workspace as a 0-open eyesore — there was no "this work is done, archive the whole container" affordance. V3.189 adds two additive fields (`bucket.archived: boolean`, `bucket.completed_at: ISO string`), the Mark-complete + Restore buttons on both PMR's Open Tasks and on each Tasks-page bucket header, and an Archived-buckets collapsed `<details>` section on both surfaces. Refactors PMR's `renderOpenTasksBlock` from project→flat-rows to project→**bucket**→rows per the wishlist's "organize as main task buckets → subtasks" — `loadOpenTasks` now populates `state.bucketSummaries` alongside `state.openTasks` so buckets with zero open subtasks still render their header + Mark-complete button. New `renderBucketGroup(summary, rows)` per-bucket framed group; new `renderArchivedBucketsSection(summaries)` collapsed accordion. `markBucketComplete` does a targeted read-modify-write on `userData/{uid}/buckets/{projectId}` (the per-project doc in the buckets subcollection) — single-doc update rather than `api.save('tasks/buckets.json')` rewriting the whole subcollection. Owner-only for V1; admin cross-user mark-complete deferred because `loadOpenTasks` already short-circuits for cross-user view (per-user buckets aren't admin-readable per the recursive `userData/{userId}/{document=**}` rule). `tasks-buckets.js` gets a matching inline "✓ Mark complete" button on each bucket header when `rollupBucket(b).open === 0 && rollupBucket(b).done > 0 && !b.archived`, plus `renderArchivedBucketsForProject` collapsed at the bottom of each task builder with restore. `sw.js` `CACHE_VERSION` 156 → 157. Wishlist's PMR-bucket-lifecycle item moves to Done. Plan: [`CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.187_pmr_bucket_lifecycle.md`](../ClaudesPlan/V3.187_pmr_bucket_lifecycle.md). ALSO V3.184.1: Camera-selection training catalog expanded 16 → 24 cameras. User flagged that the initial V3.184 catalog under-represented Tucsen and skipped Thorlabs entirely, and asked specifically about the Tucsen Libra 25 (25 mm BSI sensor — credible Iris 15 alternative on the sensitivity axis). Libra 25 spec confirmed via vendor page WebFetch: 5.3 MP, 7.52 µm pixels, 25 mm diagonal, 92% peak QE BSI, 1.0 e- read noise, <0.01 e-/pix/s dark current, 14/16-bit, USB 3.0, TEC-cooled to 0 °C. Broader sweep of Tucsen's lineup on `tucsen.com/scmos/` + Thorlabs' Zelux / Quantalux / Kiralux families followed. [`rm/js/training-camera-selection.js`](../../rm/js/training-camera-selection.js) `CATALOG` reorganized into three tiers (budget ≤$5k / mid $6-16k / premium $18-35k) and grew from 16 to 24 entries. New entries: **Tucsen Libra 25** ($14k, 25 mm BSI, 92% QE, 7.52 µm pixels, 1 e- read noise — direct Iris-15-on-sensitivity alternative); **Tucsen Aries 6504 Pro** ($19k, 4.2 MP BSI 95% QE, 0.43 e- read noise — Fusion-BT-class flagship); **Tucsen FL-26BW** ($13k, 26 MP BSI Sony IMX571, cooled to −25 °C, ultra-low dark current — chemiluminescence + long-exposure dim fluorescence); **Tucsen FL-20BW** ($7k, 20 MP cooled CMOS Sony IMX183, sub-1 e- read noise — cheapest "real" 20 MP fluorescence camera); **Tucsen Dhyana 95V2** ($14k, 4 MP 11 µm BSI GSENSE400BSI — Sona competitor with large pixels); **Thorlabs Zelux CS165MU** ($700, 1.6 MP CMOS Sony IMX273 — cheapest catalog entry, brightfield only); **Thorlabs Quantalux CC215MU** ($5k, 2.1 MP cooled sCMOS, 1 e- read noise, 61% QE — cheapest cooled scientific sCMOS); **Thorlabs Kiralux CC126MU** ($4k, 12.3 MP cooled CMOS Sony IMX304 — mid-budget well-stained fluorescence). Cheapest catalog entry drops $2k → $700. Tab 1 worked-example rewritten around the three-trade structure (same-FOV-better-sensitivity = Libra 25; same-FOV-cheapest = Dhyana 4040; smaller-FOV-premium-sensitivity = Fusion BT / Prime BSI Express) instead of one answer. Tab 5 background lists the new lineup. Budget slider min lowered $2k → $500 + step $1k → $500 so Thorlabs Zelux is reachable. Empty-state copy updated: cheapest fluorescence-capable now Quantalux CC215MU at $5k. Plan doc: catalog section appended with "V3.184.1 expansion" block listing the 8 additions (net 16 → 24) and rationale. `sw.js` `CACHE_VERSION` 155 → 156. Plan: [`CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.184_training_camera_selection.md`](../ClaudesPlan/V3.184_training_camera_selection.md).)

V3.188 (Paper-reader citation finder rewritten for comprehensive style coverage. User reported three papers where the left-rail "Citations on page N" panel was empty or undercounting: a Nature Sci Reports paper using Vancouver superscript numerals with no brackets, a Springer CMLS paper using bracketed/round-paren Vancouver numbers, and a Frontiers paper using APA author-year with semicolon clusters. Pre-V3.188 [`rm/js/paper-citations.js`](../../rm/js/paper-citations.js) only knew the `[N]` bracket and single-cite `(Author, YEAR)` patterns, so superscripts, round-paren numerics, multi-cite clusters, multi-year cites, and narrative cites all silently dropped. V3.188 refactors `_scanPage` into six independent sub-scanners that run in sequence and de-dupe at the end: `numeric:bracket` (existing `RE_NUMERIC`, refined to allow padding `[ 12 ]`), `numeric:paren` (new `RE_PAREN_NUMERIC`, gated by new `_isParenNumericInProse` helper to reject enumerated list markers and equation-number references), `numeric:super-unicode` (new `RE_UNICODE_SUPER` matching runs of `[⁰¹²³⁴⁵⁶⁷⁸⁹]` plus connectives, translated to ASCII via new `_translateSuperscripts`), `numeric:super-geom` (geometric detector picking text items whose `height < 0.78 × medianH` AND whose text is a digit-cluster AND whose preceding item is body-height and ends in a letter or closing bracket/paren — catches Nature PDFs where supers extract as plain digits in smaller-font spans), `authoryear:single|cluster|multiyear` (new `RE_PAREN_GROUP` matches the whole `(…)`, splits inner on `;`, runs non-global `RE_AY_INNER` per segment, scans trailing `, YYYY` for same-author multi-year cites; surname char class extended from `À-ſ` to `À-ɏ` to cover Latin Extended-A + Extended-B), and `authoryear:narrative` (new `RE_NARRATIVE` matches `Author et al. (YYYY)` and multi-year `Author et al. (YYYY, YYYY)` with the author sitting outside the parens). A dedupe pass at the end collapses matches sharing `(page, refKey)` whose rects overlap — needed because unicode-super + geom-super can both fire on the same span and cluster + narrative can overlap on the same cite. New `kindLabel` field per match drives a per-page diagnostic `console.info('[paper-citations] p.N matches: kindLabel=count …')`; set `window.__PAPER_CITATIONS_DEBUG__ = true` to log even on pages with zero matches. `_matchedRef` and the sidebar render paths required zero changes — all numeric variants still emit `kind: 'numeric'` and all author-year variants still emit `kind: 'authoryear'`, with `kindLabel` purely diagnostic. Out of scope for V3.188: roman-numeral cites (`[ii]`, `[IV]`), letter footnotes (`[a]`, `^b`), and bare-digit-after-word cites with no separator markers in normal body height — the last would risk a false-positive flood. `sw.js` `CACHE_VERSION` 154 → 155. Plan: [`CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.188_citation_styles_comprehensive.md`](../ClaudesPlan/V3.188_citation_styles_comprehensive.md). ALSO V3.187: Training: Daily Research Workflow in RM. Ships the V3.178-planned training module that walks new lab members through how the four daily-research pages — activity tracker, lab notebook, library, protocols — connect to each other and feed PMR. The card was previously greyed out in [`rm/pages/training.html`](../../rm/pages/training.html) as "coming soon — see plan V3.178"; V3.187 flips it `available: true` and lands the module page + JS. [`rm/pages/training-rm-daily-research.html`](../../rm/pages/training-rm-daily-research.html) (~590 lines) is a 10-section lecture-style page mirroring the V3.177 RM Essentials layout (header / TOC / sections / callout / submit-checklist / rm-quiz). [`rm/js/training-rm-daily-research.js`](../../rm/js/training-rm-daily-research.js) (~310 lines) drives three interactive widgets plus the standard TOC scroll-spy / picker-style quiz / progress-bar checklist pattern: (1) **Workflow flowchart** — six clickable nodes (tracker, library, projects, tasks, notebook, protocols) with `activeFlowSlug` state; clicking a node renders a detail panel describing that page's role in the daily flow + the downstream signals it produces (e.g. notebook → tracker pending entry + PMR figure reviews via V3.161). (2) **Tracker entry confirmer** — three mock pending entries with `rm-notebook` / `rm-protocol` / `rm-task` source pills mirroring the real V3.160 banner shape; ✓ / ✗ buttons confirm or dismiss; a live mock "PMR Activity" summary below fills in as you go, grouping confirmed hours by top-level category. (3) **Notebook entry simulator** — title + date + a sample microscopy image rendered as an inline SVG data URI (no Storage dependency) with a 📌 PMR badge; clicking toggles flagged state on the image AND reveals a preview card mimicking the V3.161 figure-review block that would appear in the student's next PMR. All three widgets are stateful but **not persisted** to Firestore — purely pedagogical, mirroring the V3.184 SNR calculator pattern. The 5-question quiz covers the five most-confusable cross-page-integration moments (notebook → auto-pending tracker, 📌 image flag, Coming-up lane for future-dated subtasks, 🧮 protocol calc in notebook, empty-PMR-Activity diagnosis). The 11-item Daily-rhythm checklist mirrors the V3.177 toolkit-checklist pattern with progress bar + completion line ("✅ Daily-rhythm adopted — start V3.179 (Tasks & PMR) next"). [`rm/js/training.js`](../../rm/js/training.js) `MODULES` entry for `rm-daily-research` flipped `available: false → true`; the rm-hub category goes 1/5 → 2/5 available. `sw.js` `CACHE_VERSION` 153 → 154. Note: this work was planned as V3.178 (filename `V3.178_training_rm_daily_research.md`) but the V3.178 version slot was already used on 2026-05-11 by the unrelated grants-facilities-biosketch ship, so the actual release takes V3.187 (next free version above V3.186). The plan filename stays unchanged; the `planVersion` field in `training.js` stays `'V3.178'` since it references the plan doc, not the ship version. Plan: [`CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.178_training_rm_daily_research.md`](../ClaudesPlan/V3.178_training_rm_daily_research.md).)

V3.186 (PMR Goals section — student writes short- and long-term aims across four time horizons. Pre-V3.186 PMR had no goals surface; the multi-year arc had no anchor, so the 1:1 conversation jumped from week-to-week activity to "are we still aligned?" with nothing in between. V3.186 adds a `Goals` block at index 0 of `renderBody`'s block list (frames everything below) with four rich-text cards: This semester / This year / PhD journey / Beyond PhD. New `GOAL_HORIZONS` constant in [rm/js/pmr.js](rm/js/pmr.js) lists the horizons with `{key, label, hint, placeholder}` strings; new `emptyGoals()` helper produces the four-key object used by both `emptyDoc()` (fresh docs include goals) and `hydrateDoc()` (backfills missing horizons on older docs). Each card has a contentEditable body (focus clears placeholder, blur restores it if empty; in-memory `state.doc.goals[horizon.key].text_html` updates on every `input`, blur stamps `last_updated`), a header chip showing last_updated, and a `💬` toggle that mounts a per-horizon comment thread via `buildCommentToggle(card, 'goals', horizon.key, ...)`. The section also has its own `section_id='goals', target_id=null` thread for cross-horizon discussion. Read-only-view guards inherited via `isReadOnlyView()` — admin viewing a student's PMR sees the goals but cannot edit them. `sw.js` `CACHE_VERSION` 152 → 153. ALSO V3.184: Training: Selecting a Camera for the Inverted Microscope. New module in the Microscopy category, slotted after V3.167 image-train — image-train teaches optics up to the sensor; this one teaches the sensor itself plus the buying decision. Triggered by an external "Iris 15 alternative" question that the user fields repeatedly when collaborators ask which camera to buy for the Ti-E / Ti2-U at a stated budget. Five-tab interactive notebook following the V3.167 image-train pattern; reuses every CSS primitive already in [`rm/css/training.css`](../../rm/css/training.css) so no new styles. [`rm/pages/training-camera-selection.html`](../../rm/pages/training-camera-selection.html) (~370 lines) + [`rm/js/training-camera-selection.js`](../../rm/js/training-camera-selection.js) (~720 lines). **Tab 1 Sensor Types** compares the 5 sensor families (Industrial CMOS / sCMOS front-illum / sCMOS BSI / qCMOS / EMCCD) with a per-family pixel-cross-section canvas showing where photons hit (front-illum: ~25-30% reflect off the wiring layer; BSI: 90-95% reach the silicon; EMCCD: gain register adds √2 excess-noise factor). **Tab 2 Specs Deciphered** walks the 6 datasheet specs that actually decide (QE / read noise / pixel size / sensor size / dynamic range / frame rate) with per-spec visual canvases (QE vs wavelength curves for front/BSI/industrial, pixel-readout-histogram for 3 read-noise tiers, same object on 3 pixel pitches, sensor footprints to scale, bit-depth gray-level bars, log-fps comparison). **Tab 3 Modality Matcher** swaps spec priorities for 10 imaging modalities (brightfield, phase / DIC, epi-well-stained, epi-dim, TIRF, spinning-disk, live-cell time-lapse, calcium, single-molecule, TFM bead tracking) — each modality has a `pri: {qe, rn, px, fov, dr, fps}` vector on a 0-3 scale, a recommended sensor family, a budget band, and a canonical-pick string; the priority canvas renders 6 weighted horizontal bars. **Tab 4 SNR Calculator** drives 6 sliders (signal photons, background photons, QE %, read noise e-, dark current e-/s, exposure s) into `SNR = (S·QE)/√(S·QE + B·QE + Nread² + D·t)`, identifies the dominant noise term (signal-shot / background-shot / read / dark), and renders a simulated noisy 80×36 pixel grid of a Gaussian object using Box-Muller noise + a seeded `mulberry32` PRNG so the image is stable across redraws of the same parameters. **Tab 5 Capstone** is a budget slider ($2k → $40k) + modality dropdown + 3 priority toggles ("large FOV / fast / lowest noise") that scores all 15 catalog cameras against the chosen modality's Tab-3 priorities and renders a printable top-5 shortlist with 2025-2026 ballpark prices. Catalog covers Photometrics Iris 9 / Iris 15 / Prime 95B / Prime BSI Express / Kinetix, Hamamatsu ORCA-Spark / Flash 4.0 V3 / Fusion BT / Quest 2 / Lightning, Andor Zyla 4.2 PLUS / Sona 4.2B-11 / iXon Ultra 888 EMCCD, Tucsen Dhyana 400BSI V3 / 4040 V2, FLIR Blackfly S USB3. Scoring: `qeScore = (qePeak−50)/15`, `rnScore = (6−rn)/1.9`, `pxScore = 3 − |pxUm−6.5|×0.5`, `fovScore = (diagMm−13)/6.5`, `drScore = (bits−12)/1.5`, `fpsScore = log10(fps)/log10(800)×3`, all clamped to 0-3, weighted sum against modality priority vector + bonuses from the toggles − industrial-CMOS-into-fluorescence and EMCCD-when-not-needed penalties. Prices flagged as ballparks needing live quotes. No new CSS, no Firestore writes, no per-user state — pure client-side reference module. `rm/js/training.js` `MODULES` gains the new microscopy entry (slug `camera-selection`, icon 📷, `available: true`, `planVersion: 'V3.184'`); Microscopy category 4→5 modules; curriculum 8→9 of 21 available (Foundations 3/3, Using RM 1/5, Microscopy 5/5, Analysis 0/3, Tools 0/5). `sw.js` `CACHE_VERSION` 150 → 151. Plan: [`CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.184_training_camera_selection.md`](../ClaudesPlan/V3.184_training_camera_selection.md).)

V3.179 (Grant Writing workflow — Phase 2 of 5: Grant Writer + Budget Editor. With V3.178's biosketch + facilities-doc generators already shipping, V3.179 adds the writing tools that complete the proposal-authoring half of the workflow; per the user's directive the federal grants portal + scraper (V3.181) and AI matcher (V3.182, depends on portal data) ship later as the discovery half. **Grant writer** [`rm/pages/grant-builder.html`](../../rm/pages/grant-builder.html) is a list+editor shell mirroring `paper-builder.html` — `?grant=<id>` switches modes. [`rm/js/grant-schemas.js`](../../rm/js/grant-schemas.js) (~330 lines) defines 29 GRANT_SECTION_KINDS (NIH R01 / NSF / DOD section types with advisory `pageBudget` per kind), 10 GRANT_BLOCK_KINDS (paragraph / aim-bullets / budget-table / personnel-table / compliance-row / letter-card / figure / equation / table / list), and 5 GRANT_TEMPLATES (nih-r01 16-section / nih-r21 12-section / nsf-standard 11-section / nsf-career 14-section / blank). [`rm/js/grant-builder.js`](../../rm/js/grant-builder.js) (~220 lines) renders the list view from Firestore-native `grantDrafts` (no JSON shadow); the new-grant modal seeds `tree` via `GrantSchemas.newTree(templateId, title)`. [`rm/js/grant-editor.js`](../../rm/js/grant-editor.js) (~600 lines) is the single-author block editor with 700ms-debounced autosave to `grantDrafts/{id}.tree`. Six block renderers: paragraph (contenteditable with focus border), aim-bullets (hypothesis textarea + grid of Aim cards with title + summary), budget-table (placement marker that opens the budget modal), personnel-table (inline-editable), compliance-row (Y/N/NA radio), letter-card (placeholder; upload integration is V3.183). Cross-page integration: **+ Biosketch** + **+ Facilities** toolbar buttons attach V3.178 collection items by id and write reciprocal backrefs onto the referenced docs. **Yjs CRDT deferred to V3.183+** — V3.179 stores the section/block tree as plain JSON matching the paper writer's Y.Doc materialization, so the future Yjs upgrade hydrates the JSON into a Y.Doc on first connect without a rules or data migration (~700 lines now vs ~7000 lines cloned from paper-editor.js + paper-yjs.js). **Budget editor**: [`rm/js/grant-budget-model.js`](../../rm/js/grant-budget-model.js) (~140 lines) has pure calc functions (no DOM): `personnelCost(row, fringe, escalation, yearIdx)` = `salary × effort × (1+esc)^y + fringe`; `mtdcBase` implements NIH MTDC exclusion rules (subaward first-$25k included, rest excluded; equipment ≥excludeAbove fully excluded; subaward indirects always excluded); `idcAmount(mtdc, rate)`; `aggregateYears(years[])` rolls Y1..Y5 into the parent doc's `budgetSummary`. [`rm/js/grant-budget.js`](../../rm/js/grant-budget.js) (~420 lines) mounts the modal from the editor's budget-table block: Y1–Y5 tabs (always rendered; unsaved years are local-only), modular ↔ detailed toggle (modular hides line items + shows a $25k-step direct-cost input — NIH ceiling $250k/yr), 7 detailed-mode category cards (Personnel × Equipment × Supplies × Travel × Consultants × Subawards × Other direct) + a Settings card (publications budget / IDC rate / MTDC vs TDC / escalation). Per-year doc writes to `grantDrafts/{id}/budget/{year}`; denormalized summary writes back to `grantDrafts/{id}.budgetSummary` so the list view + editor block render totals without loading each year doc. **LaTeX export** [`rm/js/grant-export-tex.js`](../../rm/js/grant-export-tex.js) (~170 lines) walks the tree, per-block converters emit one self-contained `.tex` source (article 11pt Helvetica, 0.5" margins, geometry+graphicx+amsmath+enumitem+titlesec); BibTeX integration deferred to V3.183. **Firestore schema**: new `grantDrafts/{grantId}` collection (lab read, lab-member write, creator-or-admin delete) + `grantDrafts/{grantId}/budget/{year}` subcollection (Y1..Y5). Yjs subcollections (`yjs_snapshot`, `yjs_updates`, `presence`, `comments`) reserved as commented rule stubs for the V3.183 upgrade. `rm/js/nav.js` enables the **Grants** entry under Research dropdown (was hidden in V3.178). `sw.js` `CACHE_VERSION` 149 → 150. Plan: [`CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.179_grant_writer.md`](../ClaudesPlan/V3.179_grant_writer.md). Remaining phases: V3.181 (portal + scraper aggregating grants.gov XML + NIH/NSF RSS + SAM.gov API + per-user Pivot-RP RSS), V3.182 (AI idea→grant matcher over the portal index using `claude-sonnet-4-20250514` via the activity-tracker.js fetch pattern).)

V3.178 (Grant Writing workflow bootstrapped — Phase 1 of 5. Mirrors the Paper Writer architecture (paper-builder.html + paper-* JS stack) for proposal artifacts. V3.178 ships the two cheapest deliverables that piggyback on data RM already has: **Biosketches** ([`rm/pages/grant-biosketch.html`](../../rm/pages/grant-biosketch.html) + [`rm/js/grant-biosketch.js`](../../rm/js/grant-biosketch.js) ~520 lines) reads `cvData/{uid}` and overlays `biosketches/{uid}/variants/{vid}` to render NIH 5-page / NSF / DOD biosketches with editable Personal Statement + up to 5 Contributions to Science (NIH) or Synergistic Activities (NSF), per-variant tailoring + autosave; Positions/Honors/Publications pull live from cvData. **Facilities & Other Resources doc** ([`rm/pages/grant-facilities.html`](../../rm/pages/grant-facilities.html) + [`rm/js/grant-facilities.js`](../../rm/js/grant-facilities.js) ~430 lines) auto-aggregates the `inventory` (items with `category==equipment` OR `unit_price>=$5k`, grouped by subcategory) + `equipment` (bookable shared instruments) + `users` (personnel narrative by category) collections into five editable HTML sections (Facilities/Equipment/Computing/Office/Other Resources); user hand-edits are preserved on "Regenerate" via hash-equality check against the saved `autoSections` mirror. Both pages use new [`rm/css/grant-doc.css`](../../rm/css/grant-doc.css) — 11pt Arial body, 0.5" margins, NIH-compliant print stylesheet (`@page { size: letter; margin: 0.5in; }`). Export to Word via [`rm/js/grant-biosketch-export.js`](../../rm/js/grant-biosketch-export.js) wrapping the rendered HTML in `application/msword` (no JSZip/OOXML dependency); Print → PDF uses `window.print()`. New Firestore collections (rules in [`firestore.rules`](../../firestore.rules)): `biosketches/{userId}` + `biosketches/{userId}/variants/{variantId}` (lab read, owner write — co-PIs review but can't edit someone else's biosketch) and `facilitiesDocs/{docId}` (lab read+write, creator-or-admin delete). [`rm/js/nav.js`](../../rm/js/nav.js) gains two children under Research (Biosketches + Facilities Doc) plus a sixth default workflow seed `grant-writing` (icon 💰). Pages `grant-builder` + `grant-portal` are reserved for V3.180 / V3.179 — the workflow's pageIds list will grow then. No Yjs, no Cloud Functions, no scraper in V3.178; full roadmap V3.179 (federal grants portal + per-user Pivot-RP RSS scraper), V3.180 (Yjs grant writer), V3.181 (budget editor), V3.182 (AI idea→grant matcher) is in [`~/.claude/plans/just-like-we-have-jiggly-cupcake.md`](../../../.claude/plans/just-like-we-have-jiggly-cupcake.md). `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 148 → 149. Plan: [`CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.178_grants_facilities_biosketch.md`](../ClaudesPlan/V3.178_grants_facilities_biosketch.md).)

V3.177.1 (RM Essentials rewritten around workflow + power-user orientation. The V3.177 first pass led with "what RM is / Vanilla HTML+JS / Firestore is the source of truth / sign-in mechanics" — Alex flagged this as dead weight. The rewrite restructures [`rm/pages/training-rm-essentials.html`](../../rm/pages/training-rm-essentials.html) entirely around becoming a power user of the workflow paradigm. New section flow: workflow paradigm → 5 defaults → strip mechanics → split view → **cross-page integration** (RM's secret weapon — V3.160 activity signals, V3.161 📌 PMR figure flagging, V3.157 cross-user task assignment) → custom workflows → daily/weekly rhythm → power-user shortcuts catalogue → page inventory → power-user quiz → power-user toolkit checklist. The "What RM is" / sign-in / Firestore sections are deleted; profile material trimmed to inline parentheticals. Quiz questions rewritten to test power-user knowledge (brand-dropdown switching / auto-pending tracker entries / Cmd+K multi-pick / 📌 PMR badge / ⊟ Split / save-as-custom). Week-1 checklist replaced with 12-item Power-user Toolkit (workflow switching via brand-dropdown / trust cross-page integration / daily tracker confirmations / Split-view / drag-reordered chips / custom workflow saved / multi-pick citations / 📌 flagging / Friday PMR weekly entries / Barriers same-day capture / weekly Inbox triage / exit workflows when done). New saved feedback memory `feedback_rm_trainings_power_user.md` enforces this framing for the remaining RM trainings (V3.178–V3.181). V3.166 (Confocal (Nikon AX-R) training shipped end-to-end — the AX-R training pair is now complete. [`rm/pages/training-confocal.html`](../../rm/pages/training-confocal.html) (~520 lines) + [`rm/js/training-confocal.js`](../../rm/js/training-confocal.js) (~720 lines) implement a 6-tab interactive notebook reusing the V3.164 shared interactive-notebook CSS block: Pinhole / Scan Modes / Lasers & Detectors / Z-Stacks / Photobleaching / Session Worksheet. Tab 1 renders widefield vs. confocal side-by-side from a single 110-dot deterministic spheroid model with per-dot in-focus vs out-of-focus blur driven by a `blendFactor` argument tied to the AU slider. Tab 2 synthesizes 9 cells per-pixel with box-Muller noise whose σ ∝ 1/√SNR; scan-mode + averaging drives per-pixel dwell, per-frame time, SNR (`√N × mode-base`), and per-frame bleach. Tab 3 plots emission-spectrum gaussians on a 380-720 nm axis with the four AX-R laser positions banded; crosstalk = pairwise Bhattacharyya-like overlap × 0.1 for sequential acquisition vs. ×1 for simultaneous; tiers safe / caution / unsafe at 5% / 15% bleed. Tab 4 computes `Δz_Nyquist = λ_em / (2·NA²·n)` for the three AX-R objectives and renders an XZ Gaussian PSF with red-dashed sampling lines. Tab 5 models bleaching with exponential decay (k = 0.007 × dose-per-frame, calibrated so default 10%-laser/2-μs dwell gives ~100-frame half-life); viability tiers at cumulative dose 12 / 30. Tab 6 worksheet issues `issues[]` blockers (missing pinhole, 5 AU pinhole, Z-step > 400 nm, dose > 70) vs `warnings[]` (DAPI simultaneous, sub-Nyquist Z, galvo + high dose) and prints a session-plan sign-off sheet. `rm/js/training.js` flips `MODULES.confocal.available` true. `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 141 → 142. Plan: `CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.166_training_confocal.md`.)

Tabs/canvases/styles in [`rm/css/training.css`](../../rm/css/training.css) — new shared interactive-notebook block (~280 lines) used by all future imaging modules: `.tab-bar`/`.tab-btn`, `.tab-content` with `order: 1..4` flex rules so source order stays semantic while visual order matches pedagogy, `.background-section`, `.equation-box`, `.worked-example` (yellow callout), `.student-task` (blue callout), `.scenario-bar`/`.scen-btn`, `.panel` (2-col 280/1fr → 1-col at 900px), `.ctrl`/`.stats`/`.stat-row`, `.viz`/`.chart-row`/`.canvas-wrap{.is-light}`, `.val-verdict{.is-bad/.is-warning/.is-critical}`, `.insight`, `.signoff-sheet`/`.signoff-pass`/`.signoff-fail`, `@media print` clean-up for the printable sign-off. Capstone Nyquist target = `λ_min/(4·NA)/1000` in μm using the shortest enabled laser; sign-off doesn't persist to Firestore — printed page is the record. `rm/js/training.js` flips `MODULES.microscope-basics.available` true so the landing card is a real link. `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 140 → 141. Plan: `CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.164_training_microscope_basics.md`.)

Three 1–5 sliders rescale the inner triangle in real time (`scoreToInnerVertex` linearly interpolates from centroid `(300, 340)` toward each outer vertex by `score/5`); `generateVerdict` cases on average + spread of the three scores to produce plain-language diagnosis + prescription. V3.161 (Notebook images flagged for PMR review. A student clicks the new 📌 PMR badge on any image attachment in a lab notebook entry; that image becomes a card in the student's next-due PMR period with editable caption, deep-link back to the notebook, fullscreen lightbox, per-figure threaded conversation, and a "+ Create follow-up task" button that wires through `task-assign.js` with the PMR backref. Pre-V3.161 the admin had no path to discuss the student's microscopy/gel images during the 1:1 without the student pasting them into the PMR weekly entry by hand. V3.161 flips the direction so flagging happens during normal notebook work.
