Dual-write: notebook entry gains `pmr_review_figures: [{storage_path, period_id, flagged_at, figure_review_id}]` (`storage_path` is the join key — stable across attachment reorders, unlike array indices); the matching PMR period doc gains `figure_reviews: [{id, storage_path, image_url, notebook_entry_id, notebook_title, notebook_date, caption, flagged_at}]`. The notebook page is the only writer of both fields; PMR-side edits are limited to caption text + per-figure removal. New helpers in `lab-notebook.js`: `togglePmrFlagOnFigure(idx)`, `removePmrFlagForFigure(storage_path)`, `resolveNextPmrPeriod()` (reads `userData/{uid}/pmr/_index`, picks most recent draft → most recent any → null), `writeFigureReviewToPmr`, `removeFigureReviewFromPmr`. New helpers in `pmr.js`: `renderFigureReviewsBlock` + `renderFigureReviewCard` + `resolveFigureUrl` (lazy Storage URL cache in `state._figureUrlCache`) + `openFigureLightbox` + `openTaskForFigureReview`. The figure-reviews block registers in `renderBody()` between Paper writing and Discussion. Per-figure conversation via `SECTION_COMMENTS.mount({scope:{section_id:'figure_reviews', target_id:fr.id}})`. Admin cross-view works without storage.rules changes because the student resolves the Storage download URL at flag time (their session has read access) and stores the long-lived token-bearing URL on the PMR doc — same trade-off as the existing paper-figures pattern. Notebook attachment thumbnail gets a `.pmr-flag-badge` corner button (off = dim, on = solid amber with glow + amber outline on the whole `.ln-att-thumb`). PMR-side card has a `.pmr-fig-review-card` amber-accented layout with 4/3 image box, editable caption, and a fullscreen click-to-close lightbox. `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 136 → 137. Plan: `CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.161_notebook_figure_pmr_review.md`.)

V3.160 (Seamless cross-page activity + task integration. Pre-V3.160 the only thing that wrote a tracker entry was subtask completion via `logTaskCompletionToTracker` in `rm/js/tasks-buckets.js`; everything else a user did in RM — paper editing, lab notebook entries, action items, admin task assignments from inside PMR — left no trace, so the activity tracker stayed empty and PMR had no visibility into what work actually happened that week. V3.160 introduces a shared producer/consumer model. New `rm/js/activity-signals.js` exposes `ActivitySignals.recordWork({source, sourceRef, date, text, categoryPath, hoursEstimate?})` and `ActivitySignals.recordSchedule({...})` — idempotent upsert into `trackerEntries/{uid}/entries` with `status:'pending'` and a deterministic doc id (`auto-<kind>-<id>-<date>` for work, `auto-<kind>-<id>` for schedule so moving a source's date moves the entry in place rather than duplicating). Confirmed and dismissed entries are NEVER trampled by re-fires. Producer hooks land in 5 pages (lab-notebook, tasks-buckets subtask due_date, tasks-dashboard-buckets syncAssignedToTracker, meetings addActionItem, protocols createProtocol) plus 2 cross-page consumers (activity-tracker pending banner + Coming up lane, tasks-dashboard Assigned to me pinned section). PMR gets two new blocks inserted between Weekly entries and Barriers: `renderActivityBlock()` reads tracker entries for the period range and groups by ISO week (pending entries shown with distinct amber styling so admin can ask "how long did this take?" during the 1:1); `renderStatsBlock()` aggregates confirmed `duration` by `categoryPath[0]` into a bar chart + daily sparkline. Both blocks use `SECTION_COMMENTS.mount` for threaded conversation. Cross-user admin reads work because firestore.rules' `trackerEntries/{userId}/entries` read rule extends to `request.auth.uid == userId || isAdmin() || (isLabMember() && users/{userId}.shareActivity == true)`. Tracker entry schema gains `status`, `scheduled_for`, `source_ref`, `auto_hours_estimate`, `confirmed_at`, `dismissed_at` (all additive — absent `status` reads as confirmed for legacy entries). Tasks-dashboard "Assigned to me" pinned section adds PMR provenance chips that deep-link back to `pmr.html?period={pmrPeriodId}`, plus a red `.tasks-tab-badge` on the Inbox tab counting `assigned && !done && !assigned_seen_at`. First view stamps `assigned_seen_at` via a batched write and fires `rm:assigned-inbox-changed` to drop the badge. Three layered confirmation surfaces (per-page toast on auto-create with Undo, inline pending banner on tracker daily view with one-tap hours + ✓/✗, and pending visibility in PMR Activity) maximize the chance the user actually confirms hours. Decision: producer/consumer hooks are per-page calls to a shared helper, NOT a global api.save wrapper — explicit, grep-able, and lets each page own its "what counts as work" definition. Admin-side task assignment can't directly write the student's tracker (firestore.rules deny cross-user trackerEntries writes); materialization happens on the student's session via the idempotent `syncAssignedToTracker` routine on next tasks dashboard load. `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 135 → 136. New composite indexes on the `entries` collection group: `(status, date)` for the pending-queue / period-rollup, `(status, scheduled_for)` for the Coming up lane. Plan: `CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.160_seamless_activity_tracking.md`.)

V3.159 (PMR becomes the canonical 1:1 conversation surface between admin and student. Pre-V3.159 PMR was a one-way student-authored worksheet — admin click on another lab member in the rail showed an "unsupported cross-user view" placeholder; there was no way to assign tasks from inside PMR; barriers were just flat discussion cards with no lifecycle; only the original 5 blocks; no per-section threads; no surfacing of papers the student is writing. V3.159: new `rm/js/section-comments.js` extracts the threaded-comment primitive from `paper-editor.js:1901-2047` and parameterizes it over `(collectionPath, scope)` so any feature can mount a per-block conversation pane. PMR adopts it on every block (project_overview, open_tasks, reference_links, weekly_entries, weekly_entries[wk.id], discussion, discussion[card.id], plus the two new blocks below) — every section is now commentable with one-level reply threading + resolve/reopen + delete-own-comment. New `renderBarriersBlock()` adds a first-class "things blocking my progress" section with status pill (open → discussing → resolved), contentEditable rich-text description, paste-image upload via `PAPER_FIGURES.uploadImage()` (lab-readable SHA-256-deduped), linked-task chips, "+ Assign task" button that wires through `task-assign.js`, and a per-barrier comment thread. New `renderPaperWritingBlock()` queries `papers` collection for `authorUids array-contains studentUid` AND `is_library_entry !== true` (drafts only), renders one row per draft with a status pill (drafting/review/submitted/in_revision/published), an "open in builder" link, "+ task" button, and a per-paper comment thread. `task-assign.js` accepts new optional `source:'pmr'`, `pmrPeriodId`, `pmrSectionId`, `pmrTargetId`, `titlePrefill`, `defaultAssigneeUid`, `heading`, `onCreated(task)` fields — PMR's "+ Assign task" buttons prefill the assignee to the student, stamp the PMR backref onto the resulting `userData/{studentUid}/tasks` doc, and use `onCreated` to back-link the new task id onto the originating barrier card. The cross-user admin view is now real: `loadIndex()` / `loadPmrDoc()` / `attachLiveSync()` branch on owner-vs-cross-user and use direct `firebridge.db().collection('userData').doc(uid).collection('pmr')` refs in the cross-user case (the api adapter's `_userScopePath` hard-codes the signed-in uid). `scheduleSave()` / `saveDoc()` short-circuit when `isReadOnlyView()` is true so admin's only writes are to comments + assigned tasks. New `userData/{userId}/pmr/{periodId}/comments/{commentId}` Firestore rule allows read+write by owner OR admin OR comment-author (the recursive `userData/{userId}/{document=**}` rule above would have locked it to owner-only). `pmr.html` loads `firebase-storage-compat`, `toast.js`, `paper-figures.js`, `task-assign.js`, `section-comments.js`. `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 134 → 135. Plan: `CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.157_pmr_conversational.md`. Data model: PMR doc gains `barriers[]` and `paper_writing[]` arrays; new `pmr/{periodId}/comments/{commentId}` Firestore subcollection scoped by `{section_id, target_id?}`. ALSO V3.158: Citation popover always anchors under the toolbar `+ Cite` button — including Cmd+K. Pre-V3.158 the toolbar `+ Cite` anchored at `_state.savedEl` (the paragraph) and Cmd+K anchored at `lastFocusedEditable` (also the paragraph), so the popover appeared in different positions depending on which paragraph held the caret. V3.158 stamps `id="pe-gt-cite"` on the toolbar `+ Cite` button (via a new optional `id` field on `_makeRefBtn`'s spec) and both surfaces look up `document.getElementById('pe-gt-cite')` as the anchor, falling back to the paragraph element when the toolbar isn't on the page (read-only viewer, embedded contexts). Chip-edit popover (V3.157) keeps its anchor on the clicked chip — that's the natural anchor for editing a specific citation. `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 133 → 134. No data-model changes.)

V3.157 (Citation popover gains multi-pick + click-an-existing-chip-to-edit. Pre-V3.157 it was strictly single-pick: one click → close + insert one `[@key]`. Authors needing a `[@k1; @k2]` Pandoc citation group had to type the second key by hand, and modifying an existing citation required deleting + retyping. V3.157: every popover row now has a left-side checkbox (V3.157 stamps `.pe-cite-pop-row-check`); clicking toggles selection without closing; a `.pe-cite-pop-foot` footer with `Cancel` / `Insert N` (or `Save N` in edit mode) / `Remove citation` (edit-only) commits via the new `onPickKeys(keys[])` callback, which builds `[@k1; @k2; …]` as a single Pandoc citation group. New helper `insertCitationKeysAtFocus(keys[])` (with `insertCitationAtFocus(key)` becoming a one-liner wrapper). Click-on-chip edit: `renderCitationChip(inner, srcStart, srcEnd)` now stamps `data-cite-src-start` / `-end` / `-keys` on the wrapping `.pe-cite-group`; a new delegated `mousedown` capture-phase listener on every contenteditable bound through `bindYTextToContentEditable` catches `.pe-cite-group` clicks and opens the popover in edit mode pre-filled with the existing keys. Commit calls new `replaceParagraphRange(paragraphEl, srcStart, srcEnd, newToken)` which walks text nodes to build a Range over those bytes and runs `execCommand('insertText')` (or empty for full-remove). New `_openCitationEditor(paragraphEl, yText, groupEl)` orchestrates the edit flow. Cmd+K + toolbar `+ Cite` both switched to `onPickKeys`. New keyboard: `Space`/Enter toggles active row; `Cmd/Ctrl+Enter` commits. Selected-bar at top of list shows checked keys as dismissable amber chips so multi-pick state is visible even after typing a new search query. CSS scope `:has(.pe-cite-pop-row-check)` keeps the V3.108 inventory popover (which reuses `.pe-cite-pop-row`) in its existing column layout. `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 132 → 133. No data-model changes — Y.Text still stores `[@k1; @k2]` Pandoc-style groups exactly as before.)

V3.155 (Toolbar `+ Cite` button: missing `onPick` callback meant picks silently no-op'd. The reference pickers (`+ Eq` / `+ Fig` / `+ Table`) all route through `_openPicker` → `_insertAtSavedCaret(token)`, which V3.154 fixed. The `+ Cite` button is special — it opens PaperEditor's generic `openCitationPopover`, which requires the caller to supply an `onPick(entry)` callback. Cmd+K's handler does (`onPick: e => insertCitationAtFocus(e.key)`); `_openCitationPopoverFallback` was passing only `anchorEl` and no `onPick`, so `citationPopoverState.onPick` was null and `pickIndex(i)` ran a no-op. Fixed by mirroring the Cmd+K wiring: `onPick: function (entry) { if (entry && entry.key) PaperEditor.insertCitationAtFocus(entry.key); }`. The `insertCitationAtFocus` flow uses PaperEditor's `lastFocusedEditable` + `lastFocusedCaret` + `setCaretCharOffset` to rebuild the caret fresh against the post-blur source-text DOM, so caret position is correct now that the callback actually fires. Why this wasn't caught in V3.154: V3.154's audit traced the `_insertAtSavedCaret` chain but didn't verify the citation popover received its callback — the no-op was several function hops past where V3.154's audit stopped. Panel References tab "Insert citation" button is a different path (`_insertCitationKeyAtSavedCaret` → `_insertAtSavedCaret`) and was already correct. `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 130 → 131. No data-model changes.)

V3.154 (Paper builder full insertion-path audit — drop the stale `_state.savedRange` gate at every entry point. V3.143/V3.152 fixed the leaf insertion function (`_insertAtSavedCaret`), but five callers (`_ensureCaretOrAlert` toolbar gate; panel-side `_insertCitationKeyAtSavedCaret` / `_insertTableReferenceAtSavedCaret` / `_insertFigureReferenceAtSavedCaret`; toolbar `+ Cite` action's `_openCitationPopoverFallback`) still gated on the cached Range. The Range goes stale the moment the contenteditable blurs and the editor's V3.107 `applyRendered(el, sourceText, renderFn)` swaps innerHTML — destroying the text nodes the cached Range references. The legacy fallback in those gates checked `document.activeElement.isContentEditable`, which is FALSE the moment the user clicks a panel-side button (active is the button), so the gate alerted "Place cursor first" or silently no-op'd even when `lastFocusedEditable` had a valid target. V3.154 unifies all five entry points: gate on `_state.savedEl && document.body.contains(savedEl)`, fall back to `_saveCaretFromCurrentFocus()` (which reads PaperEditor's persistent `lastFocusedEditable` + `lastFocusedCaret` tracker), then alert. `_openCitationPopoverFallback` was also doing `sel.addRange(_state.savedRange)` against the stale Range to set up the citation popover's anchor — fixed to rebuild a fresh Range from `_rangeFromCharOffset(savedEl, savedCaret)` before applying. Diagnostic table in CHANGELOG enumerates all 13 inline + reference + citation insertion entry points across the right-click context menu, the global toolbar, and the four side-panel tabs; V3.154 brings the five broken ones in line with the V3.152 leaf fix. `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 129 → 130. No data-model changes. Plan: `CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.154_full_insertion_path_audit.md`.)

V3.152 (Paper builder — three coupled fixes for "click button, item lands at start of paragraph (or doesn't appear at all)". (1) **Caret jumps to position 0**: V3.143's char-offset save was right, but `_insertAtSavedCaret` built the fresh Range BEFORE calling `el.focus()`. The focus handler ([rm/js/paper-editor.js:3003-3009](rm/js/paper-editor.js#L3003-L3009)) does `el.textContent = yText.toString()` to swap rendered HTML back to source — destroying the text nodes the freshly-built Range pointed to. Fix: focus FIRST, then build the Range against the now-source-text DOM. All five toolbar reference actions and the right-click context-menu insertion path share `_insertAtSavedCaret`, so a single fix covers six surfaces. (2) **`[!eqi:<id>]` → `[!eqi:<name-slug>]`**: V3.109 emitted opaque auto-IDs (`eq-abc123`); V3.152 emits a slug derived from the entry's user-set name (e.g. `hookes-law`). New helper `_eqiSlugFromEntry(e)` lowercases + replaces non-`[a-z0-9_-]` with `-` + caps at 60 chars + falls back to `e.id` for unnamed entries. Both the in-page renderer (`getInlineEquationById` in paper-editor.js) and the LaTeX exporter (`paragraphTextToTex` in paper-export-tex.js) gain a two-pass lookup: id first (legacy back-compat), then slugified-name match. New module-private `_slugifyEqName(name)` mirrors the slug rule. Old `[!eqi:eq-abc123]` tokens continue to render and export; new inserts use names. (3) **Reference inserts (`+ Eq` / `+ Fig` / `+ Table`) didn't land**: same root cause as #1 — same `_insertAtSavedCaret` call site. Final-final fallback (when Selection API has no rangeCount even after `addRange`) splices text in at `_state.savedCaret` (source-space offset) rather than appending to end-of-paragraph, so even the worst-case fallback respects the user's caret position. `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 127 → 128. No data-model changes. Plan: `CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.152_caret_focus_first_and_eqi_name.md`.)

V3.145 (Lab Notebook picker chip insert — third fix attempt, this time bypassing focus + selection entirely. V3.134 fixed the rangeCount=0 caret-restore case; V3.142 added a Selection-API fallback when execCommand silently no-ops; V3.145 ships a new `_appendHtmlToBody(bodyEl, html)` helper that uses `<template>.innerHTML = html` + `bodyEl.appendChild(fragment.firstChild)` directly, with **no focus management, no selection management, no execCommand**. Picker chip inserts (V3.114 inventory chip, V3.116 protocol chip, V3.134 full-protocol body, V3.137 calculator block insert + re-edit) all route through this. The `_insertHtmlAtCaret` helper from V3.142 is kept for the paste/drop image insertion path where caret position is meaningful (the user is mid-typing in the body). Trade-off: picker chips always land at end-of-body, ignoring any prior caret position — but the picker steals focus from the body anyway, so there was no caret to honour. Trade-off accepted because (a) the user's mental model is "pick → it goes in the entry," (b) appendChild can't fail across browser-version / focus-timing edge cases that depended on getSelection() being inside the body. Paper builder has a parallel symptom (likely a `sectionId` resolution issue in paper-editor's inventory picker, NOT execCommand-related since that path uses Yjs); punted to a separate investigation. `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 120 → 121. ALSO V3.144: Dashboard "+ Add Task" button now creates a task instead of navigating — pre-V3.144 the button at the top of `/rm/pages/tasks.html?tab=dashboard` was a plain anchor link to `?tab=add`, just changing tabs. User report: "the add task button on the task page is misleading currently this is just a link to the add task page. we should change this. the intent of this button is to add a task to something." V3.144 turns it into a button that opens an `openForm()` modal with six fields: Title (required), Task builder (select dropdown of all builders + `+ New task builder…` at the bottom), Due date, Hours estimate, Priority (low/normal/high/urgent, default normal), Notes. Default builder selection: `state.focusedTaskBuilderId` if set, else first non-`proj-inbox` builder, else first builder. On save: resolve the builder (creating a fresh one with a prompt if `__new__`), reuse the existing `ensureDefaultBucket(project)` to land the subtask in `buk-{id}-default`, build a fresh subtask with form values + the signed-in user as assignee, push onto `bucket.subtasks`, unhide the card if previously hidden, set `state.focusedTaskBuilderId` to the destination so the new card scrolls into view, `scheduleSave()` + `render()`. Cancel-during-new-builder-prompt throws so the modal stays open with field values preserved. New `window.DASH_ADD_TASK = { open: openAddTaskModal }` so tasks.html's `DOMContentLoaded` listener can wire the click. tasks.html: dashboard header `<a href="...?tab=add">` replaced with `<button id="btn-add-task">`; the same listener that wires `btn-assign-task` now also wires `btn-add-task`, falling back to a "still loading" alert if the dashboard module hasn't booted. No sub-bucket picker yet — V1 lands every task in the General default bucket and users re-pin via the V3.130 pin menu. Saves go through the dashboard's existing `scheduleSave()` (per-doc adapter write into `userData/{uid}/buckets/{id}`) — typical lab has 10-30 task builders so the latency is bearable; if it ever feels slow we can graft V3.130's surgical write. No Firestore rules change (the route is per-user). `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 119 → 120. Plan: `CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.144_dashboard_add_task_modal.md`.)

V3.143 (Paper builder caret-persistence rewrite — real fix for "click toolbar button, pick item, nothing happens." V3.140's fallback chain populated `_state.savedEl` correctly, but the bug was in `_state.savedRange`: caching a `Range` object across the focus-loss boundary broke because the contenteditable's `blur` handler runs `applyRendered(el, sourceText, renderFn)` which does `el.innerHTML = renderFn(...)` — destroying the text nodes the Range references. The picker's search input grabs focus ~30 ms after the picker opens → paragraph blurs → DOM re-renders → saved Range now points at detached nodes → `sel.addRange(savedRange)` silently fails → `execCommand('insertText')` no-ops. Same shape as V3.142's lab-notebook fix, different module. The fix abandons Range-caching entirely: caret persistence is now `(savedEl, savedCaret)` where `savedCaret` is the char-offset within the contenteditable. New `_readCharOffset(el, endContainer, endOffset)` records it on save; `_insertAtSavedCaret` rebuilds a fresh Range against the live (post-re-render) DOM via the V3.140 `_rangeFromCharOffset(el, offset)` walker before applying selection. Both `_saveCaretFromCurrentFocus` and `_saveCaretFromEvent` (right-click context-menu path) updated. Final-final fallback in `_insertAtSavedCaret` appends the text via `el.textContent +=` and dispatches `input` if even the Selection API path fails — never silent. After insertion `savedCaret` is bumped past the inserted text so successive picks stack rather than overwrite. `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 118 → 119. No data-model changes. Plan: `CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.143_caret_char_offset_save.md`.)

V3.142 (Lab Notebook chip-insert robustness — `document.execCommand('insertHTML')` is increasingly unreliable in modern browsers and the V3.134 caret-restore fix wasn't enough for every browser/state combination. New `_insertHtmlAtCaret(bodyEl, html)` helper wraps every chip-insert callsite (V3.114 inventory chip, V3.116 protocol chip, V3.119 image-paste, V3.134 full-protocol body, V3.137 calculator block insert + re-edit) with a three-step pipeline: (1) `_ensureBodyCaret(bodyEl)` — now seeds an empty `<br>` if `bodyEl.firstChild === null` so the caret has a real node to anchor on; (2) try `execCommand('insertHTML', false, html)` and verify the body's innerHTML actually grew; (3) Selection-API fallback via `range.insertNode(documentFragment)` plus a synthetic `input` event dispatch so the body's autosave + ref-collect listeners still fire. Six callsites updated; each checks the return value and surfaces a console.warn + saving-hint on failure. The empty-body seed is the subtle gate — a fresh entry's contenteditable has zero child nodes, and both execCommand AND `range.insertNode` no-op silently when the range references "empty contents." `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 117 → 118. ALSO V3.141: Protocols Builder admin override — admin-only `✏️` button on the editor's "Created by [Name] · YYYY-MM-DD · vN" byline opens a modal that lets admins reassign `author_uid` + cached `author_name` from a `lab/users.json`-driven roster picker AND set `created_at` via a `<input type="date">`. V3.119 made these fields immutable as an application-layer convention enforced only in `protocolEmptyDoc`; this layers a deliberate escape hatch on top for imported historical protocols and author handoff. Save runs `firestore.update({author_uid, author_name, created_at, updated_at})`. Defense-in-depth: both `openAuthDateModal` AND `saveAuthDate` re-check `firebridge.isAdmin()` so console-bypass of the UI hide still hits the gate; the existing protocols rule (`allow update: if isAdmin() || ownerUid == auth.uid`) provides the third layer. No new fields, no rule deploy. `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 116 → 117. ALSO V3.140 (Paper builder fix — toolbar reference buttons (`+ Inline Eq` / `+ Cite` / `+ Eq` / `+ Fig` / `+ Table`) silently no-op'd when clicked before the user had focused into a paragraph. Root cause: V3.120/V3.136 `_saveCaretFromCurrentFocus()` only saved the caret when `document.activeElement.isContentEditable`; if focus was on `<body>` / the side panel / the title input, the save skipped, `_state.savedEl` stayed null, the picker opened (or citation popover opened with `anchorEl: null`), the user picked, and `_insertAtSavedCaret` hit its silent `console.warn` early return. Fix: extend `_saveCaretFromCurrentFocus()` with two fallbacks — (1) build a collapsed end-of-element range when the contenteditable is focused but has no selection, (2) read PaperEditor's `lastFocusedEditable` + `lastFocusedCaret` (newly exposed via `getLastFocusedEditable()` / `getLastFocusedCaret()` accessors) and reconstruct a Range via the new `_rangeFromCharOffset(el, offset)` helper. The PaperEditor tracker survives blur (set on keyup/mouseup/input listeners on every contenteditable), so even if focus moved to the side panel between typing and clicking the toolbar, recovery succeeds. Every toolbar reference action gains a leading `_ensureCaretOrAlert()` guard that opens an `alert()` instead of opening a no-op picker. `_insertAtSavedCaret(text)` calls `_saveCaretFromCurrentFocus()` as last-chance recovery before its alert path. `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 115 → 116. No data-model changes. Plan: `CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.140_toolbar_caret_fallback.md`.)

V3.139 (Protocols Builder Phase 6 — skill-driven import + component-matching wizard. New `.claude/skills/protocol-import/` skill instructs any Claude session on converting `.md` / `.pdf` / `.docx` lab protocols into a single `<slug>.protocol-import.json` artifact with the V3.113/V3.117/V3.119/V3.121 block schemas, base64-embedded images on figure blocks (`image_data_uri`), and intelligent calculation extraction (prose like "5 mL × 2% = 0.1 g" becomes a `formula` block with snake_case-named variables; step bodies rewritten to `[!var:<name>]` references so the protocol becomes a live calculator). The page-side importer (`rm/js/protocol-import.js`, ~360 lines) parses + validates + summarizes the JSON; on submit, decodes each `image_data_uri` to a Blob, uploads via `PROTOCOL_FIGURES.uploadImage` to `protocol-figures/{newId}/...`, replaces the data URI with `image_url` / `storage_path` / `hash` / `mime`, builds the doc via `protocolEmptyDoc`, writes Firestore, and auto-saves a v1 snapshot via `PROTOCOL_VERSIONS.saveVersion` labeled "Imported from <filename>". List-page gains a `📥 Import protocol` button + modal (file-picker + paste textarea + auto-suggested slug + live count preview + per-stage progress). Component-matching wizard: new `PROTOCOL_INVENTORY.findMatches(freeText, opts)` pure scoring function (cat# +15, CAS +15, name +10, vendor +8, formula +3, category +2; tie-break by stock_status); new `PROTOCOL_IMPORT.openMatcher(protocolId)` builds a modal that walks materials rows with `!item_ref && free_text.trim()` and shows top-N candidates with Accept / Pick different / Skip per row; on Apply runs every chosen match in one `yDoc.transact`. Triggered by a new `🔁 Match inventory` topbar button (always available) AND auto-opened 300 ms after editor load when the URL carries `?match=1` (set by post-import navigation, then stripped via `history.replaceState`). The editor exposes `window.__PROTOCOL_EDITOR_STATE = { protocolId, yDoc, yTree, Y, canEdit, markDirty }` so the matcher can mutate the live Y.Doc directly without coupling. No new Firestore rule, no new index, no new Storage block — V3.113's `protocols/{id}` + `versions/{vId}` rules and `protocol-figures/{id}/...` Storage block cover everything. `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 114 → 115. ALSO V3.138 (Lab Notebook bench-workflow lifecycle Phase 1) — every notebook entry now travels through `phase: 'plan' | 'running' | 'complete'`, defaulting to `'plan'` for new entries; legacy entries that predate the field hydrate as `'complete'` so the V3.110→V3.137 records don't surface running-mode UI. Schema additions on the entry doc: `phase`, `run_started_at`, `run_ended_at`, `summary`, `observations: [{id, at, text, metric?, value?, unit?, attachment_paths[]}]`. New constants `NOTEBOOK_PHASES`, `NOTEBOOK_PHASE_LABELS`, `NOTEBOOK_PHASE_GLYPHS = { plan: '📝', running: '▶', complete: '✅' }` on `notebook-schemas.js`. Index row gets `phase` + `obs_count` projections. Editor render gets four new pieces: a phase pill in the meta row (color-coded; running is amber-pulsing), an Observations panel (running + complete only) with chronological log + inline `+ Observation` button, a Summary textarea (running + complete only), and a phase-aware footer that swaps between `▶ Start run` (green, plan), `⏹ End run` (amber, running), and a "✅ run completed" inline timestamp (complete). Rail row title prefixes a phase glyph; a half-finished run pulses amber so the user sees it pending. `onStartRun` / `onEndRun` / `onAddObservation` / per-row delete handlers. Observation logging uses sequential `prompt()` calls for V3.138 (text → metric → value → unit) — minimal LOC, works identically on desktop / phone / tablet at the bench; Phase 2 will replace with an inline mini-form on a sticky FAB plus Web Speech API voice memos. **Deferred to V3.139+**: per-chip planned/actual qty + lot, per-protocol "followed as written" + deviation log, per-DOE-run actuals, plan-vs-actual diff view, ↻ Spawn next iteration, templates. ALSO V3.137: Lab Notebook Protocol Calculator — protocol-driven calc inside a notebook entry. Lazy-loads `/rm/js/protocol-formulas.js` (V3.117) on first click via a one-shot `<script>` tag; then walks the protocol's Yjs state via V3.134's existing `_loadProtocolTree` + a new `_collectFormulasFromPlainTree` plain-tree walker (mirror of `protocol-formulas.js collectFormulas(yTree)` for the dehydrated structure) and feeds the resulting formula entries to `ProtocolFormulas.buildSymbolTable(formulas, math)`. Calc modal: protocol title, one input row per variable across every formula block (booleans → checkbox, vars with units → numeric+unit suffix, otherwise text), `▶ Compute` button, outputs preview, format radio defaulting to **Steps**. Three render formats: **Steps** (walk the protocol body, route every step / note / safety / materials body string through `ProtocolFormulas.renderTextWithTokens(text, symbolTable)` so `[!var:name]` tokens become inline `proto-var` chips coloured by computed/error state); **Snippet** (one-line `🧮 Title · inputs → outputs` summary); **Table** (4-column variable / value / unit / kind). All three wrap in a single `<div class="ln-calc ln-calc-{format}" contenteditable="false" data-calc-id="..." data-calc-protocol-id="..." data-calc-format="...">` atom. New `state.doc.calc_runs[]` persists the input snapshot + output snapshot + format per insert; clicking the rendered block re-opens the calc modal pre-filled (preserves `created_at`, stamps `updated_at`). Sanitizer extension: SPAN class allowlist accepts `proto-var( proto-var-(computed|unknown|error))?` plus `data-var` + `title` attrs; DIV gets a fresh allowlist restricted to `^ln-calc( ln-calc-(snippet|table|steps))?$` with `contenteditable=false` force-pinned. Entry points: protocol picker third sub-action `🧮 calc` (with `Alt+Enter` keyboard shortcut alongside V3.134's `Enter`/`Shift+Enter`); per-row `🧮 calc` button on the V3.116 Protocols-followed panel. Wire reuse: V3.118 `.ln-modal-*` chrome, V3.119 pane click delegate (gains two new branches), V3.134 `_ensureBodyCaret` + `_loadProtocolTree`. **Architectural constraint** the user added: calc is fully **protocol-driven** — lab notebook reads, never duplicates the formula evaluator; zero protocol-side schema change. ALSO V3.136: Paper builder header redesign — V3.120's flat two-group toolbar (`B I X₂ X² <>` + `+ Eq + Fig + Table + Cite`) splits into three labeled groups: **FORMAT** (markdown styling), **INLINE** (chips that render the thing itself — `+ Inline Eq` / `+ Cite`), **REFERENCE** (pointers to numbered objects — `+ Eq` / `+ Fig` / `+ Table`). Each group has a 9 px uppercase letter-spacing-rich gray sublabel floating above the buttons, with vertical dividers between groups. New `+ Inline Eq` button calls `_openInlinePicker` (was previously only reachable via right-click → Insert inline equation). Toolbar gets a subtle `#fafafa → #f5f5f4` gradient background; buttons get white surfaces with soft borders + active-press shadows. Inline-group buttons are tinted amber to echo the chip color in prose; reference-group hover is blue-tinted to echo the picker styling. Pills get bolder text + active blue shadow. New CSS: `.pe-gt-group`, `.pe-gt-group-label`, `.pe-gt-group-row`, `.pe-gt-divider`, `.pe-gt-group-format` / `-inline` / `-reference` variants. Old `.pe-rt-group` / `.pe-ref-group` retired. New JS helpers `_makeGroup` / `_makeDivider` / `_makeRefBtn` factor the group construction. `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 111 → 112. No data-model changes. Plan: `CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.136_toolbar_groups_and_inline_eq.md`.)

V3.135 (First-author enforcement on papers — Phase 2 of V3.133, completing the user-decided rule set: (a) edit paper metadata + (b) add/remove personnel are first-author OR admin; (c) V3.126 project Members assignment + (d) paper delete + (e) set/unset first authors are admin only. New Firestore rule helper `isFirstAuthor(itemId)` alongside `isAdmin()` / `isLabMember()` / `isExtensionMember()` / `isLabContributor()` — guards existence + field-presence before the `request.auth.uid in first_author_uids` membership test. The `match /items/{itemId}` write rule splits into `create`/`update`/`delete`: create stays lab-member; update is `isAdmin() || (resource.data.type != 'paper' && isLabMember()) || (resource.data.type == 'paper' && isFirstAuthor(itemId) && request.resource.data.first_author_uids == resource.data.first_author_uids)` — the equality guard preserves V3.133's admin-only ownership of who-the-first-authors-are even when a first author is updating other paper fields; delete is `isAdmin() || (resource.data.type != 'paper' && isLabMember())`. Client side: three new helpers in projects.js — `_myUid()` (firebridge.getUser uid), `_isAdminUser()` (firebridge.isAdmin), `_canEditPaper(item)` (true for non-papers, or admin, or uid in first_author_uids); plus `_canDeleteItem(item)` (paper deletes are admin-only). `renderHeroActions(item)` hides Edit + Delete on papers when the viewer can't edit/delete and surfaces a small `.item-hero-readonly` italic-gray badge ("read-only" with explanatory tooltip). `renderPersonnelSection(item)` hides `+ Add` and per-chip remove `×` when `!_canEditPaper`. The first-author star toggle remains admin-only (V3.133), matching rule (e). All four mutation handlers (`editItemById`, `deleteItemById`, `addPersonnel`, `removePersonnel`) gain a defensive entry-time `_canEditPaper`/`_canDeleteItem` check with an alert + early return — defense in depth against stale renders. `toggleFirstAuthor` now requires the pid resolves to a roster entry with `_firebaseUid: true` before adding (status removal is unrestricted), since the Firestore rule compares `request.auth.uid` and a legacy slug wouldn't grant access. Pre-existing papers without `first_author_uids` are treated as no-first-authors-set → only admins can edit (safe default). Non-paper items unchanged. Firestore rules deploy required: `firebase deploy --only firestore:rules` from the parent McGheeLabWebsite repo. New CSS: `.item-hero-readonly`. `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 110 → 111. Plan: `CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.135_paper_first_author_enforcement.md`.)

V3.134 (Lab Notebook chip-insert bug fix + insert-full-protocol-with-materials — fixes a silent-fail in chip insertion that hit every fresh-entry workflow: `bodyEl.focus()` gave the contenteditable focus but didn't create a caret/range when `getSelection().rangeCount === 0`, so `document.execCommand('insertHTML', …)` had nothing to insert into and silently no-op'd. New `_ensureBodyCaret(bodyEl)` helper always sets a collapsed range to end-of-body when none is inside it; insertInventoryChip / insertProtocolChip / the new insertFullProtocol all route through it. New `insertFullProtocol(protocolEntry)` becomes the default action when a row is clicked in the protocol picker (the V3.116 reference-only path moves to a small "ref only" sub-action on each row + Shift+Enter shortcut). Lazy-loads Yjs from CDN, reads `protocols/{id}/yjs_snapshot/state` + applies any subsequent `yjs_updates/*`, dehydrates the tree to plain JSON via inline self-contained walkers (mirror of `protocol-blocks.js dehydrateTree/Section/Block` but inlined so the lab-notebook page doesn't pull in protocol-blocks.js's render-side deps), renders sections + blocks as HTML at the caret (steps as `<p>`, notes as `📝 Note` blockquote, safety as `⚠ Safety` blockquote, figures as `[label]` placeholders, materials as text `<ul>`, formulas as `<code>`), then walks every materials-block row and for each row with `item_ref` looks up the V3.114 inventoryState.list and inserts a real V3.114 inventory chip. Side effect: the V3.121 hazard pictograms, V3.121 location info, V3.114 Materials Used panel, and V3.118 search-rail-count all light up automatically as if the student had picked the reagent themselves. Picker hint footer rewritten with the new keyboard contract (`Enter` full / `Shift+Enter` ref only). No new Firestore rule, no new index, no new Storage block. ALSO V3.133: First-author marking on papers — Phase 1 of a two-phase change. New optional schema field on paper items: `first_author_uids: string[]`, capped at 2 entries (each value matches an entry in `personnel[]` — same legacy-slug-or-Firebase-uid shape). Default missing → empty; `openAddForm('paper')` seeds a fresh `[]` and the `undefined` sentinel for non-paper types is stripped before the surgical Firestore write. UI: `renderPersonnelSection(item)` renders a `★` toggle button before each personnel chip when `item.type === 'paper'` — gold filled = first author, gray hollow = not. Click is gated behind `firebridge.isAdmin()` (non-admins see the icons but the click is a no-op with a tooltip). When at least one first author is set, a section-level helper line surfaces "⭐ First authors have higher admin rights to this paper. *(enforcement coming in V3.134.)*" New global `window.toggleFirstAuthor(itemId, pid)` validates `item.type === 'paper'`, validates `pid ∈ personnel[]`, enforces the cap of 2, patches `_allItems` in place, and persists via `saveItemSurgical(it)` (the V3.130 single-doc path). `removePersonnel` now also strips the removed pid from `first_author_uids` to keep the two arrays in sync. New CSS: `.first-author-star` (gold filled), `.first-author-star.off` (gray hollow), `.personnel-chip.first-author` (subtle `#fffbeb` background + `#fbbf24` border so first-author rows pop). Phase 2 (V3.134) is pending a user decision on which paper actions should be first-author-only — candidates: (a) edit metadata, (b) add/remove personnel, (c) gate V3.126 Members assignment, (d) delete the paper. V3.134 will add a `canEditPaper(item, uid)` in-page helper plus an `isFirstAuthor(itemId)` `firestore.rules` helper alongside `isAdmin()` / `isLabMember()`. Server-side enforcement is NOT in V3.133 — any lab member can still write `first_author_uids[]` directly via the adapter; V3.133 is a UX-layer guard only. Plan: `CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.133_paper_first_authors.md`.)

V3.132 (Paper builder panel-first authoring + doc-side picker — figures/tables/main-equations are now authored in the side panel and *picked into* the document via the inline `+ Add` menu, not built in place. Two new top-level Y.Arrays on `paperMap`: `figure_unplaced` and `table_unplaced`, each holding Y.Map blocks identical in shape to in-doc section.children figure/table blocks (idempotent bootstrap on first connect after V3.132). New `+ Figure` / `+ Table` buttons at the top of their tabs (mirroring V3.124's `+ Equation`) create entries in the unplaced libraries; both tabs now render two lists — `Unplaced (panel-only)` with amber accent + Place-in-section action, and `Placed in document` with the existing per-row controls plus a new `Unplace` action that returns the block to the library while preserving content. The doc-side `+ Add` menu's Equation/Figure/Table options now open `.pe-doc-picker` popovers anchored to the click button: a search input, a green `+ New …` row at the top (creates fresh unplaced + immediately places at the inserter position; for equations opens the V3.124 create modal), an `Unplaced (built in panel)` section, and an `Already placed in document` section (clicking a placed row re-positions it). Cross-container Y.Map moves go through new helpers `_snapshotBlockMap` + `_hydrateBlockFromSnapshot` factored out of V3.127's reorder logic — every `placeUnplacedAt` / `unplaceBlock` / `moveBlockToPosition` call snapshots the source Y.Map (recursively walking nested Y.Text/Y.Array/Y.Map via `_snapshotYValue`), deletes the source, then attach-then-mutates a fresh Y.Map at the destination via `_writeAttrValue`. Subfigures, table cells, table caption all survive losslessly. Block finder helpers `_findFigureBlock` / `_findTableBlock` extended to search the unplaced libraries after sections.children so V3.131's panel-side editors work identically on placed and unplaced items. New `_findContainingSectionId(blockId)` walks `paperMap.sections` to support the new Unplace + Delete actions on placed rows. `PaperEquations` exports `openFigurePickerAt` / `openTablePickerAt` / `openEquationPickerAt`; `PaperEditor` exports `getUnplacedFigures` / `getUnplacedTables` / `createUnplacedFigure` / `createUnplacedTable` / `placeUnplacedAt` / `unplaceBlock` / `moveBlockToPosition` / `deleteUnplaced`. New CSS: `.pe-doc-picker` + nested rows + green `+ New …` row, `.pe-place-picker` (panel-side place-at-section popover), `.pe-fig-row-unplaced` / `.pe-tab-row-unplaced` (left-side amber accent), `.pe-fig-num-badge-unplaced` / `.pe-tab-num-badge-unplaced` (amber pill in place of the number badge). Backward compat preserved: existing pre-V3.132 in-doc figure/table blocks remain in section.children with their full attrs and continue to participate in numbering. No Firestore rules / indexes / Storage rules changes.)

V3.131 (Paper builder Figures-tab row redesign — V3.115 → V3.127's 7-section vertical stack collapses to 4 grouped sections in user-task order: Header (156×110 thumbnail with absolute-positioned `Fig. N` overlay badge in bottom-left + monospace label input + Numbered toggle + Insert reference [primary blue] / Scroll to block / Delete figure [danger red] action buttons), Caption (full-width Y.Text editor — replaces the redundant V3.115 caption-preview line + the V3.127 editor-body indirection), Sub-panels (V3.115 chip editor with refreshed bigger touch targets), Layout (collapsed `<details>` containing the upload control + width / placement / centering / alt grid). The V3.127 `_buildFigureEditorBody` indirection is bypassed; controls inline directly into the new row builder so all writes flow through `blk.doc.transact` without an extra hop. Delete-figure action newly available from the panel. New CSS: `.pe-fig-row-v131` + `.pe-fig-row-header` (3-column grid) + `.pe-fig-thumb-large` + `.pe-fig-num-badge-overlay` + `.pe-fig-action-btn` with `-primary` and `-danger` variants + `.pe-fig-section-label` + `.pe-fig-caption-editor` + `.pe-fig-layout-details` (custom rotating chevron). No data-model changes; redesign is purely DOM + CSS.)

V3.130 (Surgical Firestore writes + roster picker on `/rm/pages/projects.html`. Two slowness fixes + one UX fix, all rooted in the adapter's collection-save semantics. The adapter's `_saveFirestore` ([rm/js/api-firestore-adapter.js:201-285](rm/js/api-firestore-adapter.js#L201-L285)) does a full collection replace — `batch.set(coll/{id}, ..., {merge:true})` for every row in the payload + delete sweeps for absent docs, chunked at 400 ops/commit. Every "+ Add Item" / "Edit Item" / "Add Member" click on the projects page paid that cost (~5-15s for an items.json with hundreds of papers/grants/tools). Two new helpers in `rm/js/projects.js`: `saveItemSurgical(item)` writes one `items/{id}` doc via `firebridge.db().collection('items').doc(id).set(..., { merge: true })` then fire-and-forgets the JSON shadow, with full-rewrite fallback when firebridge isn't ready. `saveProjectDocSurgical(canon, row)` does the same for the canonical projects collection (papers/courses/infrastructure/tools), stamping the `type` discriminator from `canon.sourceKey` so V3.126's typed-listing queries still resolve the row. Refactored: `openAddForm` (no more re-fetch + push + full rewrite — just patch `_allItems` and surgical-write); `window.editItemById` (reads item from local cache, surgical-writes the update); `window.deleteItemById` (direct doc delete + per-doc cross-ref cleanup, typically 0-3 docs); `window.addProjectMember` + `window.removeProjectMember` (no more full rewrite of every paper to add a member to one). Mirrors V3.96's `saveProjectSurgical` pattern from `rm/js/task-quick-builder.js`. ALSO: `window.addPersonnel(itemId)` was a free-text `<input>` ("roster id or full name"); replaced with a `<select>` populated from `_roster` (every entry the item doesn't already have, labeled `Name — Role — Email — (legacy)?`) with a `+ Free-text name (external collaborator)…` escape hatch for one-off externals. Personnel intentionally accepts both legacy slug ids and Firebase uids — distinct from V3.126's Members section, which gates Firestore reads via `member_uids[]` and so accepts only Firebase uids. `window.removePersonnel` switched to surgical-save for symmetry. `firestore.rules` permissions are unchanged — `items/{itemId}` allows lab-member writes, `projects/{projectId}` allows admin writes. `sw.js` CACHE_VERSION 106 → 107. `LINKS.removeLink` / `LINKS.pinItemToDashboard` still use the legacy full-rewrite path; they're off the hot path so out of scope for V3.130. Plan: `CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.130_projects_speed_and_member_picker.md`.)

V3.129 (Task-builder cards now declare *associated categories* and surface proposed subtasks one click away — Phase 2 of V3.128. New optional field `associated_categories: string[]` on each task-builder doc (subset of the seven canonical `CAT_ORDER` values from `rm/js/yr-shared.js`); stored alongside the rest of the doc via the existing `scheduleSave()` path with no new Firestore route, rule, or index. `renderTaskBuilderHead` gains a flex-wrapped second row (`.tb-cat-chips`) of seven toggle chips, color-filled with `CAT_COLOR[c]` when active and gray when inactive. When at least one chip is on, `renderMain` renders `renderSuggestedFromCategories(tb)` between the head and the buckets list — a cyan-tinted `.buk-accordion` whose body lists every `proposed:true` subtask whose bucket category is in the active set, excluding subtasks already inside the current task-builder. Each row has a `+ add to…` button that opens `openAddToTaskBuilderMenu(anchor, row, tb)` — a destination-scoped variant of `openPinMenu` listing the current card's sub-buckets plus `+ New sub-bucket…`. Selecting a target invokes the existing `moveNodeTo(row.subtask, row.bucket, tb, target)` so the existing pin/move plumbing handles the actual mutation + `proposed:false` clearing + render. New helpers: `getAssociatedCats(tb)`, `setAssociatedCat(tb, cat, on)`, `walkProposedByCats(catSet, excludeId)` (depth-recursive across `state.doc.taskBuilders → buckets → subtasks → children`). New state field `state.suggestedCollapsed: Set<taskBuilderId>` for per-card accordion open/closed memory (in-memory only — matches the pattern of `state.expandedBuckets`). CSS additions: `.tb-cat-chips`, `.tb-cat-chips-label`, `.tb-cat-chip`, `.tb-suggested`/`.tb-suggested-head`/`.tb-suggested-body`/`.tb-suggested-empty`/`.tb-suggested-row`/`.tb-suggested-main`/`.tb-suggested-title`/`.tb-suggested-crumb`/`.tb-suggested-meta`. `.buk-proj-head` switched to `flex-wrap: wrap` so the chips row can sit beneath the existing rollup + buttons. `sw.js` `CACHE_VERSION` 105 → 106. Walker is intentionally generic — any `proposed:true` row is eligible, though today they all land in `proj-inbox/buk-inbox-unfiled` via email-review / calendar / AI-suggester. Plan: `CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.129_category_associations.md`.)

V3.128 (Renamed the bucket-tasks tier-1 from "project" to "task builder" everywhere — "project" was overloaded between the canonical research-projects collection (papers/courses/infrastructure/tools, managed in `rm/pages/projects.html`) and the user-created task-grouping cards in `rm/js/tasks-buckets.js`. The bucket-tasks tier-1 is now consistently a *task builder*. Wrap-key for the `tasks/buckets.json` adapter route flipped `'projects'` → `'taskBuilders'` in `rm/js/api-routes.js` — purely an in-memory shape change since each tier-1 entity is already stored as a separate doc at `userData/{uid}/buckets/{id}`. ID prefix `proj-` and reserved doc `proj-inbox` stay opaque (no Firestore migration). Touched: `tasks-buckets.js` (state field `taskBuilders`, `activeTaskBuilderId`, `collapsedTaskBuilders`; helper renames `findTaskBuilder` / `rollupTaskBuilder` / `renderTaskBuilderHead` / `renderTaskBuildersCol`; selectedNode shape `{ kind: 'task_builder', taskBuilderId }`; UI strings throughout); `tasks-dashboard-buckets.js` (`focusedTaskBuilderId`, `state.doc.taskBuilders`, dashboard UI strings + swatch tooltip); `tasks-archive-buckets.js` + `tasks-inbox-buckets.js` (data-field rename, sort-dropdown labels switched to "task builder" while keeping the storage *value* as `'project'` for localStorage compat); `task-quick-builder.js` (`bucketsDoc.taskBuilders`, picker labels + prompts); `links.js` (`bucketsDoc.taskBuilders` in `linkedBucketsForItem` + sibling helpers, plus a header-comment note about the V3.128 rename); `pmr.js` (`doc.taskBuilders` in the open-tasks walker, "across all task builders" header help-text); `projects.js` (the `loadAll` fallback shape + `_bucketsDoc` initial value flipped, "Linked task builders" UI label, link-modal title + select label, unlink confirm message). Local function-parameter names `(project, bucket)` intentionally kept as scope-local labels — renaming all 30+ for marginal value would inflate the diff. localStorage keys `tasksDash.hiddenProjects` and the `'project'` sort value preserved verbatim to avoid orphaning user state. `sw.js` `CACHE_VERSION` 104 → 105. Phase 2 (V3.129) — category associations on the task-builder card with a "Suggested from category" panel for one-click adds — is queued separately. Plan: `CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.128_task_builders_rename.md`.)

V3.127 (Paper builder full panel-side editors for figures/tables + deep-clone reorder — closes the V3.125 follow-ups. Doc-side figure and table blocks become read-only previews via new `pe-figure-preview-only` / `pe-table-preview-only` wrap classes; CSS hides the inline editor controls (Numbered / label / upload row / layout details / styling toolbar / +Row +Col / caption-label) so the panel is the only editing surface. Caption + cell contenteditables on the doc-side bind with `readOnly: true`. Figures tab row gains a `<details open>` editor body via new `_buildFigureEditorBody(rootEl, fig)` — upload control (`PAPER_FIGURES.uploadImage` against the live yBlock attrs), caption Y.Text contenteditable bound through `bindYTextToContentEditable` with the paragraph render contract, layout grid (Width text input, Placement select, `\centering` checkbox, Alt text input). Tables tab row gains an analogous `_buildTableEditorBody(rootEl, tab)` — booktabs/header_row checkboxes, +Row/+Col buttons, per-column L/C/R alignment buttons (writes `attrs.align`), the grid editor itself with per-cell `<div.pe-grid-cell>` contenteditables, and the caption Y.Text editor. V3.125's reorder caveat fixed: new `_snapshotYValue(v)` recursively walks Y.Text / Y.Array / Y.Map / primitives into `{__ytype, value/items/entries}` snapshots; `_writeAttrValue(parent, key, snap, Y)` is the inverse hydrator using attach-then-mutate. `moveBlockWithinSection` rewritten to use these helpers — figure subfigures, table cells, table caption Y.Text, and skeleton_items now all survive a reorder lossless. The `Edit in panel ↗` link from V3.125 is the canonical edit entry on doc-side figure/table blocks. Equation blocks unchanged — LaTeX source is naturally inline-editable.)

V3.126 (Project Members assignment moved from Lab Notebook to Projects page — V3.118's admin Members modal is removed from `/rm/pages/lab-notebook.html`; the canonical assignment surface is now the per-item Members accordion on `/rm/pages/projects.html`. `projects.js` boots with a `_canonicalProjects: { itemId → {sourcePath, sourceKey, member_uids[]} }` map built by parallel-loading all four typed JSONs (`projects/papers.json`, `projects/courses.json`, `projects/infrastructure.json`, `projects/tools.json`) — each shards the same `projects` Firestore collection by `type`. New `renderMembersSection(item)` accordion appears immediately after Personnel, only for items whose id is mirrored in the canonical collection (silently no-ops on grants / conferences / library-only papers). `window.addProjectMember(itemId)` opens `openForm()` with a select dropdown filtered to `_roster` entries tagged `_firebaseUid: true` (legacy slug-id roster entries can't be resolved to Firebase auth so they don't grant access — they remain in Personnel for human attribution); on save it read-modify-writes the canonical typed JSON. `window.removeProjectMember(itemId, uid)` confirms before deleting the entry from `member_uids[]`. The Lab Notebook stays a read-only consumer of `member_uids[]` via the V3.118 `state.assignedProjectIds` derivation; the empty-state hint and a new admin variant both deep-link to the Projects page. The propagation invariant: any page that wants to gate a feature by project membership reads `member_uids[]` either via `api.load('projects/<type>.json')` or via a Firestore `collectionGroup('projects').where('member_uids','array-contains',uid)` query — today's consumers are PMR + Lab Notebook, future consumers (page-level access controls, project-scoped task views, project-scoped notebook search) inherit the same field with no schema migration. ALSO V3.125: Paper builder block management overhaul — section-bottom `+ Paragraph / + Equation / + Figure / + Table / + Reagent` toolbar retired in favor of an inline `+ Add` button rendered between every pair of blocks (and at top + bottom) inside each section. Clicking opens a popover menu (Paragraph / Equation / Figure / Table; Reagent/equipment for Methods sections) and inserts at exactly that position via new `insertBlockAt(sectionId, idx, kind, opts)` helper on `window.PaperEditor`. Equation choice opens the V3.124 panel-side creation modal pre-targeted to insert at the popped position; Figure/Table choices create a blank block AND open the side panel on the matching tab + scroll to the new row. Block reorder controls: each block toolbar gains `↑` / `↓` arrow buttons; the existing `⋮⋮` handle becomes a real HTML5 draggable supporting drop on another block in the same section to reorder. Both arrow + drag route through new `moveBlockWithinSection(sectionId, blockId, targetIdx)` (snapshot → delete → re-insert via attach-then-mutate, same shape as V3.115's section reorder). `moveBlockUp` / `moveBlockDown` / `moveBlockBefore` are convenience wrappers exposed on `PaperEditor`. Block-render ctx now carries `sectionId` so block-side controls can dispatch without re-walking sectionsArr. Doc-side figure and table block toolbars gain an `Edit in panel ↗` link button that calls `PaperEquations.showPanel()` + `setActiveTab('figures'/'tables')` + `scrollToFigureRow/scrollToTableRow(blockId)` — the inline editor stays for back-compat but the panel is the canonical surface. New panel APIs: `PaperEquations.setActiveTab(tabId)`, `scrollToFigureRow(blockId)`, `scrollToTableRow(blockId)`, `openEquationCreateModalAt(sectionId, idx)` (which stashes a `pendingInsertSectionId/Idx` in module state for the V3.124 modal's save handler to consume). Caveat: `moveBlockWithinSection` does best-effort attrs copy — primitive attrs survive but Y.Text / Y.Array / Y.Map sub-attrs (figure subfigures, table cells, table caption Y.Text) drop on reorder; deep-clone path is V3.126 work.)

V3.124 (Paper builder side-panel polish — three coupled tweaks to the V3.115/V3.120/V3.122 right slide-out: (1) **Resizable pane.** New `.pe-pane-resize-handle` on the left edge drives `_setupResize(panel)` to set width on `mousemove` (clamped to `[280, 800]`) and persist to `localStorage.rm_paper_pane_width` on `mouseup`. (2) **Unified equation creation in the panel.** V3.109 `+ New inline` button replaced with a single `+ Equation` button at the top of the Equations tab; opens a creation modal asking for Name + LaTeX + type radio (`inline` / `main`). New Y.Map field `type` on equation library entries. New "Main equations" panel section between Inline and Numbered; each main row has an `Insert as block` button that calls `PaperEditor.addEquationToSection(sectionId, {body, display: true, numbered: true, label})` to clone the LaTeX into a new numbered display block in the section of the last-focused paragraph (resolved via `_resolveSectionIdForInsert()` walking up `.pe-section[data-section-id]` ancestors). Section-level `+ Equation` button retired — the panel is the only equation creation surface. `addEquation(sectionId, opts)` extended to accept body/display/numbered/label so the panel-side flow can pre-populate. (3) **References tab UX.** Cited and Linked sections wrapped in `<details open>` collapsible elements with a custom `summary::after` chevron. The V3.122 read-only `unknown` badge for unknown-key citations replaced with a clickable red `+ Add to library` pill that opens a small modal (cite key pre-filled read-only, Title required, First author / Year / DOI optional); save creates a stub items.json entry with `meta.library.is_library_entry: true` + `stub_created_from_paper_builder: true`, then invalidates + reloads the citation index so the row flips to `cited` (green) within ~1 s. No new schema beyond the `type` field on equation library entries; no new Firestore paths.)

V3.123 (Lab Notebook DOE builder — Phase 2 of the ELN roadmap. New `🧮 DOE` toolbar button opens a 720 px modal with a design-type radio row (Latin Hypercube / Full Factorial / Central Composite), a factors editor (name / low / high / levels-for-factorial), per-type config (LHS run count; CCD center-point replicates with auto α = (2^k)^(1/4)), generate/preview/insert workflow. Algorithms in-house: `_doeFullFactorial` (cartesian product), `_doeLatinHypercube` (stratified-random with stratum-center sampling, no jitter — reproducible), `_doeCentralComposite` (2^k corners + 2k axial + n₀ centers). Run caps: 200 LHS, 256 factorial, 6 CCD factors — keeps the in-table preview readable and the entry doc well under 1 MB. Stored as structured `state.doc.design = {type, factors[{name,low,high,levels?}], runs[{factorName: value, ...}], config:{nRuns?,n0?,alpha?}, created_at}` — Phase 3 will overlay actuals onto each run without migration. New Design panel renders below the body and above the Protocols-followed panel; sticky-header monospace matrix table inside a max-height-320px scroll box; `✏️ Edit / Regenerate` + `⬇ Download CSV` (client-side Blob, named `<entryId>_<type>.csv`) + `Clear` actions. Schema: `notebookEmptyEntry` / `notebookHydrate` initialise/coerce `design`; `notebookIndexRow` exposes `design_runs` count for future rail-row signalling. No new Firestore rule, index, or Storage block — pure entry-doc field. ALSO V3.122: Paper builder References tab — closes the V3.115/V3.120/V3.122 plan. Replaces the placeholder References tab with three sections (Cited / Linked / Library search) plus a search input that filters the lab's full citation index. Each row renders a status badge (`cited` green / `linked` amber / `library` gray / `unknown` red), a deep-link title to `/rm/pages/library-paper.html`, author/year/journal meta, and per-row `Insert citation` + `Link to draft` / `Unlink` buttons. New `getCitedKeysInPaper()` walker traverses `paperMap.sections → children` for every `[@key]` token across paragraph bodies, V3.115 skeleton bullets, V3.102 figure captions, and V3.120 table-caption Y.Texts. `loadCitationIndex` projection extended with `linkedDrafts: lib.linked_drafts.slice() || []` so the panel filters "linked but not cited" without a second items.json fetch. `_toggleLinkToDraft` writes `meta.library.linked_drafts[]` via the standard `api.load`/`api.save` path (same as V3.106 reader-side picker), then invalidates + reloads the cached index so the row's status flips immediately. Lazy-load: first References-tab open triggers `loadCitationIndex()` and shows "Loading lab library…" placeholders; the listener on `paper-citations-loaded` re-renders when the index resolves. No new schema, no new Firestore paths.)

V3.121 (Lab Notebook GHS hazard warnings + location info on inventory chips — chip pipeline now stamps `data-inv-hazard-signal`, `data-inv-hazard-pictos`, `data-inv-locations`, `data-inv-storage` from the inventory record at insert; a sibling `<span class="ln-inv-hazard">` containing a Danger/Warning pill plus per-code GHS pictogram badges renders immediately after each chemical chip (`contenteditable=false`, sanitizer allowlists the new class + data attrs). The Materials panel becomes a two-line row layout (main: kind/name/meta/hazard, sub: 📍 location · 🌡 storage) with a graceful italic "📍 location not set" hint + an admin-only `(set)` link to `/rm/pages/inventory.html?focus=<id>` since 0/935 inventory items have populated `locations[]` today. New `openSafetyDetailsModal` shows the full SDS-style breakdown — H-codes, P-codes, exposure routes, PPE chips, NFPA 704 diamond (4-quadrant rotated CSS, blue/red/yellow/white), first-aid (inhalation/skin/eye/ingestion paragraphs), storage, locations, MSDS link — and reuses the V3.118 `.ln-modal-*` chrome unchanged. Picker popover row meta now shows the Danger/Warning pill + pictograms inline next to the name (highest-value placement: warning visible before pick) and a `📍 N location(s)` count when populated. `_projectInventoryEntry` projects the full safety record from `inventory/chemicals.json` plus `locations[]` from both inventory sources; `collectInventoryRefs()` derives `inventory_refs[]` from chip data attrs so the entry-level array stays in sync with the body. ALSO previous V3.121 — Protocols Builder Phase 3 — inventory linking on materials rows + inline `[!inv:<id>]` tokens in step / note / safety / figure-caption bodies. New `rm/js/protocol-inventory.js` (~270 lines) clones the lab-notebook V3.114 picker pattern: `ensureList()` loads + caches `inventory/items.json` + `inventory/chemicals.json`, dispatching a `protocol-inventory-loaded` document event on completion so bindings + materials cells re-render once the data arrives. `openPicker({onPick, anchorEl})` floats a search popover with ↑/↓/Enter/Esc keybinds, kind badges, and stock chips. `renderTextWithTokens(text)` substitutes `[!inv:<id>]` occurrences with chip spans. Materials block: each row's item cell has two modes — when `item_ref` is null, free-text `<input>` + `🧪` picker button; when set, a chip showing the item's display label + a colored stock chip + an unlink `×`. Picking auto-fills `free_text` so unlinking later still leaves human-readable text. Step / note / safety / figure-caption block controls gain a `🧪` button that opens the picker and inserts `[!inv:<id>]` at the end of the body Y.Text. `bindYTextWithVars` extended to substitute `[!inv:…]` tokens alongside V3.117's `[!var:…]` in a single sorted-by-offset pass. Stock chip is a 4-color severity scale (`full` green / `low` amber / `out` red / no chip otherwise). Tech debt: lab-notebook + paper-editor + this module are the three live copies of the inventory-picker pattern — flagged for a follow-on `rm/js/inventory-picker.js` extraction. ALSO V3.120: Paper builder pill-tab bar + global toolbar + Tables tab — V3.115 single panel-toggle button replaced with a persistent pill bar at the top of the editor (text-only Equations / Figures / Tables / References, no icons); active pill = active tab in the right slide-out, click again to close. Below the pills, a single global toolbar consolidates V3.107's per-paragraph rich-text buttons (B / I / X₂ / X² / `<>`) and the V3.109/V3.115 reference inserts (+ Eq / + Fig / + Table / + Cite). Each toolbar button uses `mousedown.preventDefault` to keep the focused contenteditable's selection across the click; reference buttons restore the saved caret before opening a picker. Tables tab fully wired: per-table row with Tab. N badge, schema readout (`3 × 4 · booktabs · header`), label/caption preview, Numbered checkbox, Insert reference / Open block actions. Table block toolbar gains Numbered + label + Tab. # badge mirroring V3.109/V3.115; below it a styling toolbar (booktabs / header row / per-column L/C/R alignment); below the grid, a caption contenteditable bound to a new `attrs.caption` Y.Text (idempotent migration from the legacy string). New `[!tab:<label>]` paragraph token renders as `Tab. N` chip and exports to `\ref{<label>}`. `tableToTex` rewritten to honor block-level attrs (`booktabs`, `header_row`, `align`, `numbered`, `label`) with template fallback. Per-paragraph rich-text toolbar (V3.107) retired; Cmd+B/I/E keybinds still work via `richTextKeydownHandler`.)

V3.119 (Protocols Builder Phase 5a — authorship, version control, forking. Every protocol carries an immutable `author_uid` + cached `author_name` set at creation; the editor topbar and list cards show the byline. New `rm/js/protocol-versions.js` (~280 lines) provides `saveVersion / listVersions / loadVersion / forkProtocol / migrateForkToStandalone`. "Save version" runs a Firestore transaction that increments `current_version` on the parent doc and writes an immutable snapshot to `protocols/{id}/versions/v{N}` with the dehydrated tree, meta snapshot, snapshotter uid + display name, optional label/message, and parent-version pointer. The historical-version viewer (`?version=N`) hydrates a LOCAL Y.Doc from the snapshot — same render code paths, no Firestore subscription, no edit affordances. Forks: any lab member can fork any protocol; `forkProtocol` does a transactional fork that creates a new top-level `protocols/{forkId}` owned by the forking user with `forked_from: {protocol_id, protocol_title, version, forked_at, forked_by_uid, migrated_to_standalone_at}` populated and atomically increments the source's `fork_count`. The fork-of chip displays lineage on both the editor (with link back to the parent) and list cards. "Migrate to standalone" stamps `forked_from.migrated_to_standalone_at` rather than nulling the field, preserving the lineage record while hiding the badge. Firestore rule `match /protocols/{protocolId}/versions/{vId}` — lab-member read or public read on parent's `status == 'published'`; only owner/admin create with `snapshot_by_uid == request.auth.uid`; immutable updates; admin delete only. Composite index `(forked_from.protocol_id asc, created_at desc)`. Schema bumped to `schema_version: 2`; legacy V3.113 docs default the new fields via `protocolHydrate`. ALSO V3.117: Protocols Builder Phase 2 — formulas with live variable propagation. New `rm/js/protocol-formulas.js` lazy-loads mathjs from CDN on first formula render and exposes a per-protocol symbol table built by walking every `formula` block. Inputs from `attrs.variables[]`; outputs from evaluating `attrs.expression` against the running scope (outputs of earlier formulas visible to later formulas — chained calculations work). Inline `[!var:<name>]` token in any step / note / safety / figure-caption body resolves to a `<span class="proto-var">value unit</span>` chip on blur; raw text is shown on focus so the literal token can be edited. Renderers refresh chips on a `protocol-vars-changed` document event without a full tree re-render. Schema for the new `formula` block: `{label, description, expression, variables: Y.Array<Y.Map<{name,label,default,unit}>>, output: Y.Map<{name,label,unit,decimals}>}`. mathjs-now-Pyodide-later per the user-locked roadmap; `python` block kind remains reserved for a later phase. Also: V3.113 polish — `canEdit` is consistently honored across all block renderers (read-only viewers see no controls and disabled inputs); seed-race guard ensures non-owners opening a brand-new protocol wait for the owner's first Yjs flush instead of double-seeding the tree. ALSO V3.116: Lab Notebook protocol references — new toolbar button `📋 Protocol` opens a search popover over the lab-shared `protocols` Firestore collection (sorted by `updated_at` desc, status badge per row); a click inserts a real `<a class="ln-protocol-chip" href="/rm/pages/protocols.html?protocol=<id>" data-protocol-id="..." data-protocol-title="...">📋 Title</a>` at the caret. Body sanitizer's `ALLOWED_ATTRS` extended for `A` to permit `class`, `contenteditable`, and `data-protocol-{id,title}`; the class attr is post-strip-restricted to the literal `ln-protocol-chip` and `contenteditable=false` is force-pinned. New entry-level `protocol_refs[]` array is fully derivable from chips in the body — `collectProtocolRefs()` runs after every keystroke and pre-save; chip deletion in prose flows back to the array automatically. Protocols-followed summary panel renders between the body and the materials panel; rail rows show a `📋 N` count alongside `📎 N` and `🧪 N`. The picker reuses the V3.114 `.ln-inv-popover` styling unchanged. No new Firestore rule, no new index, no new Storage block — the V3.113 lab-shared `protocols/list.json` route and the blanket `userData/{uid}/{document=**}` rule cover the read paths and the new field respectively. ALSO V3.115: paper builder tabbed right pane + Figures tab — V3.109 standalone Equations slide-out refactored into a four-tab pane (📐 Equations / 🖼 Figures / 📋 Tables / 📚 References), with the Equations tab carrying the existing inline-library + numbered-equations sections unchanged. Figures tab ships fully wired: per-figure thumbnail row with caption preview, label input, Numbered toggle, and a free-text subfigure chip editor (auto-letter A/B/C…). Right-click context menu in paragraph bodies extended to four items (added 🖼 Insert figure reference and 📚 Insert citation); the figure-reference picker is a two-step popover (figure with thumbnail → optional subfigure / range like `(A-C)`). Three new paragraph tokens — `[!fig:<label>]`, `[!fig:<label>:<panel>]`, `[!fig:<label>:<a>-<b>]` — render as `Fig. N`/`Fig. N (A)`/`Fig. N (A-B)` chips and export to `\ref{<label>}` with `\,(A)`/`\,(A--B)` suffixes. Figure block toolbar gains Numbered checkbox + label input + Fig. # badge mirroring the V3.109 equation pattern. Tables and References tabs render placeholder empty states until V3.116 / V3.117. Module symbol stays `window.PaperEquations` for back-compat; events fire on both `paper-equations-changed` (legacy) and `paper-panel-changed` (V3.115). No schema migration — pre-V3.115 figure blocks default to numbered=true with empty subfigures.)

## System Overview

McGheeLab website is a single-page application (SPA) using vanilla HTML, CSS, and JavaScript. It uses hash-based routing to navigate between sections without page reloads. Public site content is stored in content.json, while user-generated content (stories, profiles) lives in Firebase Firestore. Authentication is handled by Firebase Auth with invitation-based registration.

As of V3.40 the site is moving toward a **two-tier shape**: the public marketing SPA at `/` (mission, research, projects, team, classes, news, opportunities, login) and the auth-gated **RM** dashboard at `/rm/`. RM is the single home for every lab tool — the 12 standalone apps under `/apps/` and the public-site dashboard/admin/cv/guide routes are migrating into RM in three phases: **Phase A (V3.40)** stitches every existing lab app into RM via iframe-bridge wrappers and prunes the public **Lab Apps** menu; **Phase B (V3.41–V3.52)** ports each app onto the RM data contract (`api.load`/`api.save`, IndexedDB cache, `LIVE_SYNC`, `firebridge` auth gate); **Phase C (V3.53)** deletes `/apps/` and the public-site admin/dashboard surface entirely. Plan: [CodeLog/ClaudesPlan/V3.40_rm_app_migration.md](../ClaudesPlan/V3.40_rm_app_migration.md).

## Module Breakdown

### index.html
- **Purpose:** HTML shell with persistent header, navigation, hero, and footer
- **Key elements:** `#app` container swapped by router; three nav surfaces: `#desktop-nav` (inline horizontal, ≥768px), `#bottom-tabs` + `#more-sheet` (mobile tab bar, <768px), `#site-nav` drawer (legacy, hidden)
- **SEO:** Meta description, keywords, OpenGraph, and Twitter Card tags
- **User system nav:** Dashboard, CV Builder, Admin, Login/Logout links (conditionally visible based on auth state)
- **Script loading:** Firebase SDK (compat, including messaging) → Chart.js CDN → firebase-config.js → user-system.js → cv-builder.js → scheduler.js → class-builder.js → lab-apps.js → push-notifications.js → app.js (all deferred, in order); SW registration inline script at end of head
- **PWA:** Manifest link, theme-color, iOS PWA meta tags, favicon links, Microsoft Tile meta

### app.js
- **Purpose:** SPA router, page rendering, and UI interactions
- **Key functions:** Hash-based route matching with multi-segment support (`#/dashboard/story/:id`), dynamic content injection, expandable stories, mobile touch support
- **User system integration:** Routes for login, dashboard, cv, admin, logout, schedule; wires user-system page interactivity; appends Firestore stories to projects page; loads news feed from Firestore; hides hero on CV page
  - **Scheduler routes:** `#/dashboard/scheduler/{id}` (owner admin view), `#/schedule/{id}?key={uuid}` (private speaker access, key-only)
- **PI CV view:** `buildPiExpandedHTML()` renders collapsible `<details>` sections with filter bar, citation badges, year-sorted items; `wirePiCvFilter()` handles chip-based section filtering
- **News page:** `renderNews()` renders feed layout + filter-bar slot; `buildNewsFeedCard()` creates cards (now uses `buildExpandedHTML()` so team renders alongside sections); `wireNewsFeedInteractions()` hooks up social features using prefetched comment counts
  - **Filter bar:** `buildNewsFilterBar()` builds Type/Person/Time/Has-comments controls from loaded posts; `wireNewsFilters()` owns filter state and re-renders from the cached list via `applyNewsFilters()`; helpers `collectNewsPeople()` / `postInvolvesPerson()` unify author + team (author/mentor/contributors) for the Person filter
  - Comment counts prefetched once via `Promise.all(getCommentsByStory)`; stored on each post as `_commentCount` for instant has-comments filtering and badge rendering
- **Image lightbox:** `ensureLightbox()` lazily builds a single shared overlay in `<body>`; `openImageLightbox(src, alt)` opens it; `wireImageZoom(rootEl)` delegates clicks on images inside research-stories/news feeds. Supports wheel/double-click/pinch zoom and drag pan; closes on backdrop, X, or Escape
- **Error handling:** Render functions wrapped in try/catch with user-facing fallback messages

### firebase-config.js
- **Purpose:** Firebase initialization with project credentials
- **Graceful degradation:** If credentials are placeholder values, logs a warning and skips init — site works without Firebase

### user-system.js
- **Purpose:** All user system logic — authentication, Firestore CRUD, image processing, dashboard, story editor, admin panel
- **Key sections:**
  - **Media utilities:** Client-side image resize via Canvas API → three webp resolutions (thumb 300px, medium 800px, full 1600px); video upload (mp4/webm, up to 50 MB) direct to Firebase Storage
  - **DB operations:** CRUD for users, stories, news posts, project packages, classes (course listings), invitations, comments, reactions collections
  - **CV-to-profile sync:** `syncCVToProfile()` maps CV builder data (journals, conferences, presentations, patents) to public user profile fields with year, citations, journal metadata, and other rich fields
  - **Auth:** Login (15s timeout via Promise.race), invitation-gated registration, Google popup login (15s timeout), logout, auth state management (`_authStateResolved` flag, `onChange`/`offChange` listener subscribe/unsubscribe), navigation updates
  - **Dashboard:** Profile editing (name, bio, photo, category), story list with create/edit/delete, scheduler management (create/list/delete, manage via scheduler editor)
  - **Scheduler editor:** `renderSchedulerEditor`/`wireSchedulerEditor` — admin/owner view of a scheduler using `McgheeLab.Scheduler` engine; `renderSchedulePage`/`wireSchedulePage` — private key-only speaker access
  - **Story editor:** Multi-section editor with text areas, drag-and-drop image/video upload, reorder/remove sections, live preview modal, save draft / publish / submit for review
  - **News editor:** Simplified story editor for news posts — title, category (Event/Conference/Paper/Highlight/Lab Life/Other), cover image, Team section (author readonly, mentor dropdown, contributor chip picker — mirrors story editor), sections with text + media, draft/publish flow. Saves `team: { author, mentor, contributors }` alongside legacy `authorName`/`authorUid`/`authorPhoto` for backwards compatibility
  - **Admin panel:** Tabbed interface — user management (role changes), invitation generator (with copy link), pending story review (approve/reject), pending news review (approve/reject), course listings (add creates both `classes` + `schedules` docs with auto-generated schedule ID; edit navigates to course builder; delete cascades across participants + classFiles + Storage + schedules)
- **Exports:** All render/wire functions and Auth/DB objects on `window.McgheeLab` namespace

### cv-builder.js
- **Purpose:** Full-featured academic CV builder — data management, import/export, analytics, AI assistant
- **Key sections:**
  - **Schemas:** 11 section types (profile, education, employment, journals, conferences, presentations, patents, grants, awards, teaching, service) with typed field definitions
  - **Parsers:** BibTeX (basic + context-aware filename detection), NSF Current & Pending, NSF Biosketch, duplicate detection
  - **Generators:** LaTeX (moderncv template), BibTeX export, PDF HTML (3 templates: classic, modern, NSF)
  - **External APIs:** CrossRef (DOI auto-fill, citation counts), ORCID (public works search), Anthropic (AI writing assistant via per-user API key)
  - **State management:** `_data` object stored in Firestore `cvData/{uid}`, auto-syncs associations to user profile on every save
  - **Versioning:** Named snapshots with per-section and per-entry visibility toggles
  - **Dashboard/Analytics:** Completeness stats, section counts, Chart.js charts (publications timeline, category breakdown)
- **Render/wire pattern:** `renderCV()` returns HTML shell; `wireCV()` loads data from Firestore, populates from user profile on first load, attaches all event listeners
- **Exports:** `McgheeLab.renderCV`, `McgheeLab.wireCV`

### scheduler.js
- **Purpose:** Standalone, reusable dual-mode scheduling engine — embeddable anywhere on the site (class pages, events, conferences). Zero module state; all state passed via config object.
- **Key sections:**
  - **Unified builder layout:** `builderHTML()` / `wireBuilder()` — calendar on left + scrollable day-column time grid on right; both modes share same layout; grid has one column per selected day with date headers + sticky headers; 7am–9pm, 15-min rows
  - **Two scheduling modes:**
    - **Sessions:** Admin sets session duration (15m–2hr) via dropdown, clicks grid cells to place/remove blocks of that length per day; `sessionBlocks: [{ id, day, start, end, color }]` — one block per day
